# Mod Atlas (text index)

Generated 2026-11-21 16:30 from MOD_ATLAS.html in this folder (open it in a browser). This file is for agents to grep: every view, node, code location and log line.
Subsystem views come from the first atlas build; "Detailed flow" views come from the six area maps (notes/mod-atlas/parts/*.json), merged by notes/mod-atlas/merge_into_atlas.py.
When you change code: update the affected part JSON and/or the subsystem view, re-run `python -I notes/mod-atlas/merge_into_atlas.py`, and log it on SESSIONS_BOARD.md.

## Whole-mod decision map  `#system-map`  (Start here)

Follow the standalone core, native game services, and optional Traffic Addon from frame admission through decisions and output. Select a node to open its subsystem graph. Installed build: core 7EA65297, Traffic 77271F50 (FVO_RELEASE=OFF diagnostic).

- **SFSE and game state** (action, `main_vehicle.cpp:565`): The game's runtime and callback interfaces determine whether the FVO core starts and which update path it owns.
- **Can this frame touch the world?** (decision, `main_vehicle.cpp:347`): The frame scheduler combines callback cadence and world safety. A load can admit limited arrival work under cover, while ordinary simulation waits for a safe full update.
  - in Frame: World safe for the full simulation (!loading && worldReady)?: 2026-11-15 hotfix: no full simulation at the main menu, during a save load or its settle. Door/fast-travel/landing screens without a save load keep the full update. Transitions log and leave a crumb.
  - in Frame: Run Full update, Housekeeping, or nothing?: Full is downgraded to Housekeeping when the world is not safe. Skip runs nothing more except the pacing measurement.
  - in Frame: Frame(): main-thread task entry each SFSE task pass: trafficprofile::Report first (outside the re-entry guard), then the guard, heartbeat, load drop, world state, scheduler.
- **Housekeeping and load services** (state, `main_vehicle.cpp:384`): Paused or unsafe polls can process selected bindings, reporting, and load coordination. Eligible arrival placement goes through the traffic and native service boundaries.
  - in Frame: Housekeeping: features, bindings, log flush, traffic rush: Paused or held frames: features::Tick, bindings::Tick(false, worldSafe), logger::Service; trafficpool::RushTick only when not loading and world ready. Never scans the scene or queues AI work.
- **UI, controls and route authoring** (action, `main_vehicle.cpp:429`): Input and native Scaleform views collect player intent, configuration, route edits and scanner orders. Route drafts and working copies supply authoring state.
- **Vehicles, crews and world evidence** (action, `main_vehicle.cpp:328`): Core subsystems publish world state, route surveys, navigation and loaded vehicle facts. NPC and passenger jobs supply active roles and input.
  - in Update: Vehicle update enters area D: Order in the full update: npcvehicles::BeginFrame, vehiclehits::Tick, (map/nav), npcvehicles::Tick + passenger::Tick, vehicleflight::Tick, then realvehicles, ringroad, trafficpool::Tick, scantarget, traffic::Tick, vehiclekeep::Tick. On loading-screen arrival frames only world::Publish, navseed and trafficpool::Tick run.
  - in Update: 1 identity+world: world::Publish (the vehicle list): Cost bucket 0. Every later step reads this frame's vehicle list.
  - in Update: Vehicle frame task: features::Tick then bindings::Tick: Full updates call features::Tick(); bindings::Tick(); before world::Publish and the flight tick, and bindings::EndUpdate() at the end. Housekeeping frames (paused/loading) call bindings::Tick(false, worldSafe): samples kept, no events published, no game reads without a world.
- **Traffic addon attached?** (decision, `main_vehicle.cpp:382`): The native bridge negotiates the addon interface. The addon schedules population and journeys using core facts and asynchronous authority.
- **Select vehicle control and motion mode** (decision, `main_vehicle.cpp:334`): Manual inputs, Cruise, NPC jobs and Traffic all reach vehicle control. Ground and hover have distinct outputs and native application paths.
- **Population, handoff and journey choices** (action, `main_vehicle.cpp:335`): The addon decides which vehicles to admit, release, dispatch, or retire, based on profiles, route availability and current lifecycle evidence.
- **Drive and look ahead** (action, `main_vehicle.cpp:334`): Cruise and AI use route/goal planning and wheel control. The installed build includes the four-corner correction for eligible vehicles on wheels.
- **Hover, fly and land** (action, `main_vehicle.cpp:334`): Pilot or player controls become hover commands and physics output. Ground evidence and settling govern the handoff back to wheels.
- **Execute guarded native requests** (action, `lifecycle.cpp:55`): Lifecycle work can run in the native service or under cover with ownership checks. The controllers apply live steering and flight through their designated physics paths.
  - in Submit: Loading screen up and cover thread known?: Needs the native service arena verified (Compatible: 15-byte check at 1422CDF60). Screen up: keep the ticket in the CoverList. Otherwise hand off any cover leftovers, then the native queue.
  - in Submit: Command runs: inside the service (owner thread)?: Wrapped work calls crash::Action, then refuses (result 0) if not InService (threading flag clear or owner == this thread). Request::Run skips canceled or old-epoch work.
- **Save or restore the named game state** (state, `main_vehicle.cpp:131`): Traffic saves are addon-dependent. The core also handles the player's hover record and save-load reset.
  - in InstallLoadHook: Hook the game's load routine FUN_141815620: SFSE never sent PreLoadGame/PostLoadGame in practice, so the load routine itself is wrapped (15-byte prologue check). Not installed: FVO does not pause during loads.

Arrows: sfse → frame (admitted update); frame → house (load / not full); frame → interface (input / tools); frame → scene (full world update); house → traffic (arrival permitted); interface → traffic (route/profile commands); interface → move (player intent); scene → move (world, crew and route facts); scene → traffic (population evidence); traffic → trafficwork (compatible); trafficwork → move (journey / handoff); trafficwork → service (birth or retirement); move → ground (wheels); move → hover (flight); traffic → save (attached, save/load); sfse → save (save/load hooks)

## Startup and frame authority  `#runtime`  (Core operation)

The core admits one update owner. Save loading and world readiness govern access to live game state.

- **SFSE loads the core** (action, `main_vehicle.cpp:565`): Initialize logging, telemetry and crash capture, then validate the game runtime and SFSE interfaces.
- **Runtime, listeners and early setup ready?** (decision, `main_vehicle.cpp:571`): SFSE load returns false for an unsupported runtime, missing/failed interfaces, or early listener preparation failure.
- **Core initialization refused** (state, `main_vehicle.cpp:501`): The relevant startup path disables FVO updates and records why when a logger is available.
- **Seamless core API received?** (decision, `main_vehicle.cpp:518`): PostLoad registers the listener. A received API must have valid data, size and version. A supplied incompatible API prevents the standalone alternative.
  - in CoreMessage: Core API message valid (type SPAP, version 1, full size)?: Invalid: g_started = true and FVO disables itself so no second update hook is ever installed. Valid: WithCore.
- **Attach to host callbacks** (action, `main_vehicle.cpp:478`): Install the vehicle physics hook and feature systems, then register save, frame, player-update and reset callbacks with the host.
  - in InstallFeatureSystems: InstallFeatureSystems: every feature hook, once: world::SetServices (vehicle list callbacks), then hoverui, routeedit, mapguard, disableguard, inputgate, mappoints, loadedarea, npcvehicles, profiles, vehicleaim, vehiclediag, vehiclehits enemy bar, flighttune::Load. Each Install checks its own bytes and logs on mismatch; none can stop the service.
- **Install standalone hooks** (action, `main_vehicle.cpp:499`): PostDataLoad falls through to standalone when no core started. Require the task interface, native update probe and physics hook. Add a permanent task; install the standalone save hook.
  - in WithoutCore: PostDataLoad: start the standalone service: The normal path. Needs the task interface, then vehicle_probe::Install (ship-system update hook 142169100: ground probes and the native update pulse) and vehicleflight::Install (vehicle step hook).
  - in WithoutCore: Ship-system update hook installed (pulse + probes)?: vehicle_probe::Install hooks addr::kShipSystemUpdate (16-byte prologue). Its detour posts updateschedule::Pulse each native update and services ground/ray/sweep probes inside the game's update. Missing: 'standalone update unavailable; disabled'.
  - in WithoutCore: Vehicle physics step hook installed?: vehicleflight::Install hooks addr::kVehicleStep 1423CB260 (plus VTOL level/damp/clamp and boost hooks). Missing: FVO disabled. Owned by area B.
- **Frame callback admitted?** (decision, `main_vehicle.cpp:347`): An atomic guard skips recursive callbacks. Heartbeat is independent of the simulation cadence. Poll the scheduler using the native update pulse.
  - in Frame: Frame(): main-thread task entry each SFSE task pass: trafficprofile::Report first (outside the re-entry guard), then the guard, heartbeat, load drop, world state, scheduler.
- **World loading or invalid?** (decision, `main_vehicle.cpp:356`): Reset held world state on real replacements. Track manager state, save-load interval and world readiness before granting world actions.
  - in Frame: Held: 1 s load trace; re-attach kept vehicles after load: Once a second: loading, world ready, screen, manager state, TES interior cell, hook counts. After the load routine returned and world ready: vehiclediag::AfterLoadTick gives kept vehicles a chassis before the world runs.
  - in Frame: Planet manager state: 0/1 world replaced, 2 world ready?: game::ReadPlanetState (vtable-checked). Standalone: world ready and state 0/1 -> Reset. State 3/4 (terrain re-centre every ~250 m) never resets. g_worldReady = state 2.
  - in Frame: World safe for the full simulation (!loading && worldReady)?: 2026-11-15 hotfix: no full simulation at the main menu, during a save load or its settle. Door/fast-travel/landing screens without a save load keep the full update. Transitions log and leave a crumb.
- **Arrival placement and housekeeping** (action, `main_vehicle.cpp:384`): A qualifying arrival can publish world facts, seed navigation and tick Traffic. Otherwise full work is downgraded to housekeeping during an unsafe world.
  - in Frame: Behind the screen during a save settle: vehicle list + traffic only: When arrival && loading && work != Skip: world::Publish, navseed::Tick (nav map seeded first), trafficpool::Tick. Flight, AI and everything else wait.
  - in Frame: Housekeeping: features, bindings, log flush, traffic rush: Paused or held frames: features::Tick, bindings::Tick(false, worldSafe), logger::Service; trafficpool::RushTick only when not loading and world ready. Never scans the scene or queues AI work.
  - in Frame: Run Full update, Housekeeping, or nothing?: Full is downgraded to Housekeeping when the world is not safe. Skip runs nothing more except the pacing measurement.
- **Scheduler grants a full update?** (decision, `main_vehicle.cpp:417`): A safe world still needs a full scheduled pulse. Housekeeping can refresh bindings/logs; duplicate polls skip that work.
  - in Frame: Run Full update, Housekeeping, or nothing?: Full is downgraded to Housekeeping when the world is not safe. Skip runs nothing more except the pacing measurement.

## Native service and vehicle registry  `#service`  (Core operation)

Core lifecycle requests run through the native queue or the loading-screen service. The registry supplies vehicle identity and protected references.

- **Discover and register vehicles** (action, `realvehicles.cpp:688`): Scan loaded cells in bounded work, discover vehicle bases and retain registered references and road inventory.
  - in Tick: Vehicle registry and road inventory: Records every real vehicle in loaded cells with place, owner and user (vehicles.tsv), clears stacked duplicates; RefreshRoadInventory/AppendRoadVehicles give the addon the road's existing vehicles; placements and summon points. · log `traffic.road_inventory`
- **Is the reference eligible?** (decision, `realvehicles.cpp:710`): Placement management checks the specific owned/tracked identity. Protected references have additional exclusions in the disable path.
- **Leave the reference alone** (state, `lifecycle.cpp:55`): A failed admission, unsupported queue, unavailable reference or wrong service owner prevents the requested mutation.
  - in Submit: Loading screen up and cover thread known?: Needs the native service arena verified (Compatible: 15-byte check at 1422CDF60). Screen up: keep the ticket in the CoverList. Otherwise hand off any cover leftovers, then the native queue.
  - in Submit: Command runs: inside the service (owner thread)?: Wrapped work calls crash::Action, then refuses (result 0) if not InService (threading flag clear or owner == this thread). Request::Run skips canceled or old-epoch work.
- **Post or poll an operation** (action, `lifecycle.cpp:153`): Use the keyed request table for acceptance, pending state, deduplication and completion. Own any captured reference with Keep.
  - in PollRequest: lifecycle::Poll / Post: keyed request to run engine work: Requests table (lifecycle_requests.h): one ticket per key, 64 limit, epoch generation so results from an old world read as 0. Poll returns the result on a later frame; Post is fire-and-forget. No synchronous waits. · log `OBS system=lifecycle.poll`
- **Native queue usable?** (decision, `lifecycle.cpp:55`): Submit first checks allocator compatibility and availability of the service object.
  - in Submit: Loading screen up and cover thread known?: Needs the native service arena verified (Compatible: 15-byte check at 1422CDF60). Screen up: keep the ticket in the CoverList. Otherwise hand off any cover leftovers, then the native queue.
  - in Submit: Command runs: inside the service (owner thread)?: Wrapped work calls crash::Action, then refuses (result 0) if not InService (threading flag clear or owner == this thread). Request::Run skips canceled or old-epoch work.
- **Loading screen and known cover thread?** (decision, `lifecycle.cpp:69`): Use the reduced-step queue only when the screen is up and its main thread has been identified.
  - in Submit: Loading screen up and cover thread known?: Needs the native service arena verified (Compatible: 15-byte check at 1422CDF60). Screen up: keep the ticket in the CoverList. Otherwise hand off any cover leftovers, then the native queue.
  - in Submit: Command runs: inside the service (owner thread)?: Wrapped work calls crash::Action, then refuses (result 0) if not InService (threading flag clear or owner == this thread). Request::Run skips canceled or old-epoch work.
- **Reduced step can acquire service lock?** (decision, `lifecycle.cpp:94`): Only the known owner thread tries the lock. A busy step leaves commands pending. Available steps drain within the stated budget and release the lock after a fault.
- **Submit to the native service** (action, `lifecycle.cpp:37`): Once cover ends, give remaining cover tickets to the native queue before newer work. A failed handoff completes the ticket without a successful result.
  - in HandOff: Screen down: cover leftovers go to the native queue first: Called on every Poll/Post/Pending; logs commands run behind the screen, steps, longest, handed and busy.
- **Execute after service-owner check** (action, `lifecycle.cpp:60`): Even queued work rechecks ownership at execution. Completion is polled by the caller, without a re-entrant traffic callback.
  - in Submit: Loading screen up and cover thread known?: Needs the native service arena verified (Compatible: 15-byte check at 1422CDF60). Screen up: keep the ticket in the CoverList. Otherwise hand off any cover leftovers, then the native queue.
  - in Submit: Command runs: inside the service (owner thread)?: Wrapped work calls crash::Action, then refuses (result 0) if not InService (threading flag clear or owner == this thread). Request::Run skips canceled or old-epoch work.
- **World reset closes out old captures** (state, `lifecycle.cpp:180`): Reset advances the observation epoch and clears the request and cover collections. Close and Open are separate lifecycle controls.

Arrows: scan → protect (reference action); protect → ask (eligible); protect → reject (rejected); ask → compatible (new work); compatible → reject (no); compatible → cover (yes); cover → reduced (yes); cover → queue (no); reduced → execute (lock acquired); reduced → reduced (busy → later step); reduced → queue (screen ended); queue → execute (dequeued); execute → ask (read completion); ask → reset (load/reset)

## NPC drivers and passenger control  `#crew`  (Core operation)

NPC jobs manage boarding, driving and exit. Passenger control uses the existing pilot or driver without changing the physical seat.

- **Update NPC job slots** (action, `npcvehicles.cpp:5762`): The NPC tick updates active jobs and forwards driving input for eligible following jobs.
- **Owned job still has valid actors and vehicle?** (decision, `npcvehicles.cpp:3499`): Update resolves the references and checks eligibility before using them. Cold-start traffic has separate loaded-model handling.
- **Boarding job** (action, `npcvehicles.cpp:3784`): The job advances its mount workflow and waits for a seated driver before entering Following.
- **Following, escort or assigned drive** (action, `npcvehicles.cpp:3862`): The following phase evaluates job role and orders. Driving or flight remains owned by the existing vehicle interfaces.
- **Settle or leave** (action, `npcvehicles.cpp:4198`): The exit phases settle the job, then watch departure. End/cleanup releases the job's active authority.
- **Passenger binding or native passenger seat** (action, `passenger.cpp:463`): The passenger tick can adopt a confirmed native passenger seat or request boarding through the ride-along action.
  - in Tick: Player passenger ride and control: The player rides a vehicle an NPC job flies; the NPC's autopilot drives (2026-10-28: no taxi steering from the seat). Exit and take-the-wheel stay on the passenger keys; Steer is the taxi goal when the controller is Taxi.
- **Still in the same vehicle?** (decision, `passenger.cpp:407`): If the engine ended the seat, tear down the passenger control state. Arrival or a blocked native exit may also invoke GetOut.
  - in Tick: Player passenger ride and control: The player rides a vehicle an NPC job flies; the NPC's autopilot drives (2026-10-28: no taxi steering from the seat). Exit and take-the-wheel stay on the passenger keys; Steer is the taxi goal when the controller is Taxi.
- **Control swap requested with a driver?** (decision, `passenger.cpp:445`): G returns control if already held. Otherwise a known pilot or a native driver must exist before passenger control can be acquired.
  - in Tick: Player passenger ride and control: The player rides a vehicle an NPC job flies; the NPC's autopilot drives (2026-10-28: no taxi steering from the seat). Exit and take-the-wheel stay on the passenger keys; Steer is the taxi goal when the controller is Taxi.
- **Publish passenger driving input** (action, `passenger.cpp:455`): While in control, ControlTick forwards player input through the vehicle's shared control paths and retains the NPC at the wheel.
  - in Tick: Player passenger ride and control: The player rides a vehicle an NPC job flies; the NPC's autopilot drives (2026-10-28: no taxi steering from the seat). Exit and take-the-wheel stay on the passenger keys; Steer is the taxi goal when the controller is Taxi.
- **Clear control or request safe exit** (action, `passenger.cpp:202`): Where exit is needed, validate a safe boarding site or defer it. Losing the game seat clears the record immediately.

Arrows: jobs → valid (NPC job); valid → board (boarding phase); valid → following (following phase); valid → settle (invalid / exit phases); board → following (seat verified); following → settle (departure / job end); jobs → pass (parallel passenger tick); pass → still (seat tracked); still → out (no / exit); still → toggle (yes); toggle → control (takes control); toggle → still (no swap / returns control); control → still (next tick)

## Rover summon and hover restoration  `#rover`  (Core operation)

Summoning chooses a position and validates it before relocation. Hover restoration follows the saved vehicle and height through a separate path.

- **Queue rover relocation** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:3428`): AskMove rejects wrecked or disabled rovers. For summons it prefers a nearby authored summon point; otherwise it starts behind the player with a long ground probe.
- **Ground site safe?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:3502`): Wait for fresh ground evidence or the timeout, then ask the environment for a site verdict. Retries around the player apply to non-authored pickup sites.
- **Try another ground site** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:3522`): Reposition the candidate and issue a new probe for up to two alternate sites around the player.
- **Out-of-view air spot available?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:3466`): After ground candidates are exhausted, try positions behind and around the camera. Reject visible/predicted-visible, solid or too-close spots.
- **Relocate with valid rover and worldspace** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:3537`): A valid exterior and rover are required. Ground moves use the safe site's floor; a successful air move sets follow for the return trip.
- **Cancel or leave pending as appropriate** (state, `vehicleflight.cpp:3482`): Missing rover/worldspace or exhausted air positions ends the request; a pending probe may wait for a later callback.
- **Write the named save's hover state** (action, `flightsave.cpp:42`): Record vehicle, altitude and gear after resolving the save key. Remove a previous record for a reused slot when hover is off.
  - in OnSave: Save or load event (game hooks): SaveGame/LoadGame hooks in main_vehicle.cpp (area A), installed only with the Traffic addon.
  - in OnSave: Save: hovering by hand? Write the record, else delete an old one: FlightStates\<key>.txt next to FVO settings: 'FVO saved hover v1', vehicle, z, gear. Key = save name lower-case without .sfs.
- **Read the corresponding hover record** (action, `flightsave.cpp:65`): Clear old state on load and parse the named file. Missing or unreadable files yield no pending restore.
  - in OnLoad: Save or load event (game hooks): SaveGame/LoadGame hooks in main_vehicle.cpp (area A), installed only with the Traffic addon.
  - in OnLoad: Load: read this save's record as a pending request: Forgets the old world's hover; unreadable or missing record = load as before.
- **Restore verdict permits takeoff?** (decision, `flightsave.cpp:83`): Wait for the matching driven vehicle and height criteria within the restore window. Consume the pending result on terminal success or refusal.
  - in TakeRestore: Out: restore answered (PlayerTick starts the hover): 
- **Restart flight with saved gear** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:7659`): The flight tick consumes a successful restore and applies gear after Start succeeds.
  - in PlayerTick: Hover off: does a loaded save ask for the hover back?: Asked every frame while hover is off until answered. Restore: g.gear = saved gear, then Start(driven) through the same on path as the hover key.

Arrows: ask → ground (candidate); ground → move (safe); ground → retry (retry available); retry → ground (next probe); ground → air (unsafe / exhausted); air → move (yes); air → giveup (exhausted / invalid); move → giveup (reference/world/engine refusal); save → read (later load of same save); read → restore (valid record); restore → restore (wait); restore → start (yes); restore → giveup (refused)

## Cruise and destination admission  `#cruise`  (Movement and flight)

Player Cruise uses the addon’s route claim and core planning. Auto, scanner trips and NPC pilots can also enter the planner with their own goals.

- **Cruise toggle** (action, `scantarget.cpp:566`): The scanner/input tick resolves the Cruise trigger and sends it to the core while the player is driving.
  - in Tick: Scanner marker and T-key gestures: Scanner open: ray distance and T sets the map marker, chaining the own vehicle (summon / follow / Auto to marker). targetinput: tap selects, double tap clears, hold arms a ram; targetsession keeps the marker identity. · log `input.key`
- **Player driving, with a route provider?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:8467`): Running Cruise toggles Auto off. Starting Cruise requires a driven vehicle and an addon planner; a failed Auto start is also a refusal.
- **Manual control or Cruise refusal** (state, `vehicleflight.cpp:7630`): Leaving the seat or ending Auto drops the cruise claim and pilot navigation. W/A/S/D, gear or brake can take over.
  - in PlayerTick: Auto on and a drive key, gear or brake pressed?: Accelerate/Brake/TurnLeft/TurnRight amount > 0 or the gear key ends Auto (playerAuto, autopilot off). Boarding with a pending scanner trip instead starts Auto (Start on its wheels). Ending Auto also ends Cruise later in the frame.
- **Start Auto and initialize Cruise** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:8452`): Auto starts on wheels if the vehicle is not already hovering. Cruise resets the route claim, retry clock and wait position.
- **Current route claim available?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:7785`): A missing claim goes through the addon callback when the retry clock permits. A failed search delays the next request five seconds.
- **Find and claim a fitting lane** (action, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1692`): Filter by world, city, route and accessibility, then require a fitting road lane when bounds are known. Prefer a recent route within reach, then select a route and reserve its slot.
- **Route step supplies a goal?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:7796`): A successful step installs target coordinates, stopping radius, speed, pass-through and route navigation. A finished or failed step clears the claim and permits another request.
- **Hold at the last wait position** (state, `vehicleflight.cpp:7812`): The core sets a local hold while waiting for an available lap.
- **Other goal sources** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:7817`): Auto can use a profile goal or the custom map marker. Scanner requests enter through RequestScannerTrip, and NPC pilots through PilotFly and PilotsTick.
- **Lane, strict hold, or goal scene?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:5028`): Use an authored lane, up to two seconds of Drive lane grace on wheels, or an eligible synthesized NPC job/combat line. Strict traffic policy without a lane stands; other calls use the goal scene.
- **Lane snapshot** (state, `vehicleflight.cpp:5064`): Pass the lane window, travel policy, speed request, measured vehicle and world evidence into the lane planner.
- **Coordinator brake and hold** (state, `vehicleflight.cpp:5891`): Publish a zero-speed/brake decision and the coordinator’s route-guidance refusal reason.
- **Goal snapshot** (state, `vehicleflight.cpp:4794`): A standalone goal snapshot supplies the target, floor, shapes, travel preference and measured or fallback car model. The same planner consumes it.
  - in ProbeBlockerFloor: Floor confidence: is this blocking point under a fitted road?: Player lane: a held static ray point blocking the centre path below 2 m/s gets five down-rays 0.45 m round it (main thread). surface::FitProbedFloor clears it when the fitted reachable road lies wholly above it (error <= 0.10, grade <= carcap MaxGrade) and a connected sample is on the hit's own body or all five are connected (FC-2), with no competing lower floor. A cleared point is a verified floor point for 3 s; ApplyFloorProofs removes matching points from the lane snapshot, and Recover's Stand may return to Follow at once (verified_floor_retry; wheel command held to 1.25 m/s for 0.75 s). · log `OBS system=world.floor_confidence / OBS system=plan.floor_confidence`

Arrows: input → admit (Request); admit → off (Toggle off / refused); admit → start (Accepted); start → claim (Player tick); claim → step (Yes); claim → pick (Missing and retry due); claim → wait (Retry delay); pick → step (Claimed); pick → wait (No claim); step → scene (Goal supplied); step → wait (Step ends); wait → claim (Next eligible tick); wait → scene (Evaluate current hold); goal → scene (Autopilot goal); scene → lane (Lane available); scene → hold (Strict policy); scene → goalplan (General goal)

## World sensing and floor truth  `#world`  (Movement and flight)

Reachable floor levels, static evidence and moving users feed the planner. The source preserves separate collection, invalidation and classification paths.

- **Gather world evidence** (action, `main_vehicle.cpp:329`): The main task services survey, navmesh input and bake, box scan, flight field and navmap before calling the vehicle flight tick.
  - in Update: 3 map+navigation: bake, survey, navmesh, seed, field, surface, navmap: In order: cellbake, routesurvey, navprobe, navmesh (each told Loading()), navseed, navfield::Service, environment, navmap. Each part timed; the slowest is named in a spike line.

Housekeeping frames (paused/loading) call bindings::Tick(false, worldSafe): samples kept, no events published, no game reads without a world.
  - in Update: 6 flight: vehicleflight::Tick, vehiclediag::Tick, loadedarea::Tick: Cost bucket 5. The physics itself runs later in the vehicle-step hook on the physics thread; this is the main-thread planning side.
- **Survey the reachable floor** (action, `routesurvey.cpp:126`): Batched down rays measure route floors across the lane, and a touching vehicle can note its measured ground. Flood from the current floor and read connected levels near each wanted sample.
- **Reachable elevation available?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:5229`): The matching floor replaces the planned sample and carries the local level to the next lookup. A miss leaves the sample unmeasured. For verdict 3 ray hits with no fallback, SurfaceOf can recover a reachable floor climb..4 m above the hit (climb = carcap 0.35 m, D2 2026-11-19; was 0.25) if no lower reachable floor is in the vehicle-height band. World also uses fitted reachable floor at cached point XY during radar export. The lane scene keeps the original measured floor for collision and low-edge checks. Its grade profile may correct a suspect leading sample before extrapolating behind the car, when three contiguous forward measurements agree on a local slope and the extrapolated height is consistent with the measured car base.

- **Prediction contradicted by live evidence?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:5303`): World trust discards a prediction contradicted by a clean ray or newer measurement. With rays down, keep the prediction as a fail-safe.
- **Remove superseded prediction** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:5305`): The contradicted shape is removed. The remaining live ray shapes still go through the surface rule.
- **Single surface rule** (decision, `surface.h:101`): SB-1 checks height before face normals. A hit more than the one car record's climb (carcap 0.35 m, D2 2026-11-19; replaced surface's own 0.25 m step) below a known floor is Below; more than climb above is Solid whatever its normal. The steepest drivable face is carcap MaxGrade(grip). With no reliable normal, near-floor height is the support evidence.
- **Drop Drivable or Below ray shapes** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:5320`): Remove Drivable and Below retained lane rays. Radar also skips Below returns and hull sweeps discard those contacts without recording them as ground.
- **Retain obstacle evidence** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:5321`): Keep the remaining Ray shapes and a recent body touch. The planner still applies shape policy and geometry to decide what blocks a rollout.
- **Check doubtful and near-field evidence** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:5338`): Main-thread look-ahead probes run under frame and vehicle budgets. The eligible forward volume turns measured hits into World shapes at their own heights (E 2026-11-19: no pass-height or low-edge verdict of its own; VT-1 solids 0.1 m half). Floor confidence (ProbeBlockerFloor; log OBS system=world.floor_confidence / plan.floor_confidence): when a held static ray point blocks the player's centre path below 2 m/s, the main thread casts five down-rays 0.45 m around it. A point the fitted reachable road lies wholly above (a connected sample on the hit's own body, or all five connected, FC-2; no competing lower floor) is a verified floor point for 3 s, and ApplyFloorProofs removes matching points from the next lane snapshots. On the player's lane, hull-sweep hits with no surveyed floor stay Unknown for these checks.

- **World shapes and floor go to Plan** (state, `vehicleflight.cpp:5737`): Before a lane snapshot is handed to Plan, World rechecks exported radar points against the fitted, reachable floor at each point's own XY. It withholds a point only when it is more than one wheel step under that floor, within the existing 4 m search, and there is no measured competing floor in the vehicle clearance band. The cache retains the original evidence, so it can count again from another driving level. Existing radar shapes join the lane snapshot. Lane gathering precedes the subsequent EnergyStep radar update, so that read can reflect earlier sensing. A goal snapshot gathers its own World view after that update. The diagnostic build retains sensor labels through World-to-Plan conversion, including radar rotating rays, forward rays and hull sweeps. world.sensors counts inputs by source and measured floor samples for the exact player lane snapshot used by Plan. - **Road lanes and edge no-go bands (known, not sensed)** (state, `roadfield.h:47`): Producer: the Traffic Addon's FvoRoadLanesV1 (roadlanes.h:10) now also reports bandLow / bandHigh, the route edge no-go band widths in metres beyond the lowest / highest lateral lane edge (0 = none known). roadfield.h LanesAtFull builds the full struct (the outermost lane gives up the band on its outer side; a median is taken out of the lanes on any side that borders another lane). roadfield.h LanesAt is the size-safe wrapper the provider uses: it copies only the bytes the caller's own `size` covers (kFvoRoadLanesV1BaseSize, roadlanes.h:19, is the size before the band fields) and keeps that size, so an older core or caller with the smaller struct keeps working and simply sees no bands. Consumer: vehicleflight.cpp reads it through fieldgate::roadLanesProvider at every lane gather and plausibility-checks it. This is known road data, not World evidence: no ray, sweep or world-trust rule touches it, and it never stops a car by itself (Plan alone judges bodies against it, see plan: known no-go). Build described: core DCDD57CB + Traffic BAC5B692 (source and offline checks only; in-game proof pending).

Arrows: frame → floor (Lane/world data); frame → radar (Per-pilot radar path); floor → floorvalid (Find matching level); floorvalid → build (Measured floor); floorvalid → build (Unmeasured sample); build → trust (Predicted shapes); trust → drop (Drop contradicted); trust → judge (Keep / classify live rays); judge → clear (Drivable or Below); judge → keep (Solid or unknown); clear → probe (Next evidence); keep → probe (Next evidence); radar → radardata (Eligible); radar → submit (Skip new radar work); radardata → submit (Radar store); probe → roadfit (Shape list); drop → judge (Classify remaining live rays); roadfit → submit (Shapes with road and spread); frame → lanebands (Road lanes at the car); lanebands → submit (Lanes with bandLow / bandHigh ride in the frame)

## Energy planner, recovery and commitment  `#plan`  (Movement and flight)

The asynchronous energy planner scores lane and goal snapshots. The recovery mode filters feasible candidates before the commitment rule picks the control path.

- **Copy scene for worker** (action, `planenergy.h:513`): Lane and goal submissions copy source data. Budgeted slots check global work and their own busy flag. Player lane planning with a known car model uses its dedicated Submit path. When a verified point left the snapshot and the full-speed centre candidate is feasible, Recover's Stand returns to Follow at once (verified_floor_retry) and the wheel command is held to 1.25 m/s for 0.75 s.
  - in Queue: Snapshot submitted: Asynchronous hand-off: the result is read by step 7 when it is published.
- **Worker can accept this update?** (decision, `planenergy.h:530`): A closed budget, busy slot or failed queue leaves the most recent result available for the reader. Clear the busy guard if submission fails.
  - in FillLane: Copied snapshot handed to the plan worker: Frame, 101 line points, 24 floor samples, field boxes, shapes, bicycle state and spec. A busy worker means this update's data is not used. The lane scene keeps the original measured floor for collision and low-edge checks. Its grade profile may correct a suspect leading sample before extrapolating behind the car, when three contiguous forward measurements agree on a local slope and the extrapolated height is consistent with the measured car base. · log `OBS system=plan.cost`
- **Build lane or goal scene** (action, `planenergy.h:361`): Reset committed memory and field on vehicle or scene-kind change. Lane workers use the local route and asynchronously refresh the field. Goal workers build from destination and measured floor. The lane scene keeps the original measured floor for collision and low-edge checks. Its grade profile may correct a suspect leading sample before extrapolating behind the car, when three contiguous forward measurements agree on a local slope and the extrapolated height is consistent with the measured car base. Traffic supplies lane geometry, travel permissions, speed, hold reason and stop distance through Route guidance. World supplies obstacles and contacts. Speed and Plan process those inputs through the ordinary driving pipeline. Scene::legal (known no-go intervals) is filled here from the road lanes (see known legal intervals). Being added by another agent, not yet in the installed build: the planlattice cost-to-go field also knows the bands, so the field's lane and route cost agree with the refusal in Score. Build described: core DCDD57CB + Traffic BAC5B692 (source and offline checks only; in-game proof pending).
- **CC-1/CC-2: keep a margin from measured lane edges** (action, `corridor.h:94`): Lane scenes on wheels: corridor::Measure finds hard edges beside the lane (2 m behind to 15 m ahead, solids in the body band judged against their road) and shifts the followed line by the least amount that keeps 0.5 m; too narrow -> midway. Eased 0.6 m/s, 0.1 m hold. CC-2: each car remembers its measured edge points (Memory, 256, 0.5 m cells); they go only when passed 5 m, beyond 40 m, 30 s unseen or another level, never because they are not seen now. Log OBS system=plan.corridor shift target left right edges remembered memory why. No-go bands: Measure also counts known edges (the band and median solids built from Scene::legal; certain, never remembered) in its result field known = 0 none, 1 left, 2 right, 3 both, and ShiftRoute moves Scene::legal by the same shift as the line (known no-go space is fixed in the world; only its offsets from the moved line change). Trace plan.corridor now prints known=. Build described: core DCDD57CB + Traffic BAC5B692 (source and offline checks only; in-game proof pending).
- **Feasible spot-test pulse?** (decision, `energyplan.h:682`): An admitted health-test pulse competes through the existing scene. A refused pulse continues to ordinary candidates. For the one-bay factory lease, the held drive lane admits a bounded reverse pulse. The Controller consumes it only while its lease pulse serial matches. A below-world stock lease submits the same bounded reverse test through Goal Plan, using measured support and an expiring pulse serial. Below-world stock requires fresh wheel contact with the same native body, a local floor and a feasible reverse pulse.
- **Evaluate all movement candidates** (action, `energyplan.h:696`): Managed lanes score ordinary and recovery motion in one candidate set. Crawl, steering, reverse, and an available breadcrumb retrace compete. Static and moving contact, predicted grade, and terminal braking room raise the clearance tier. A 100-point tier step dominates the bounded preference score. Equal tiers use the original motion costs and recovery preferences. A better tier releases commitment immediately. The short wheel-clearance check evaluates the first wheel command of each ordinary rollout on the same World snapshot. Its suggested speed and reason contribute to Plan's score, including a clearance tier on managed lanes. A zero suggestion is eligible for selection. The Arbiter owns the choice and the Controller publishes its result. Wheels rollouts whose body enters a known band or median are refused (enters_a_known_band) unless the car is already inside and the move does not go deeper; see the no-go node. Build described: core DCDD57CB + Traffic BAC5B692 (source and offline checks only; in-game proof pending).
- **Update recovery context** (decision, `energyplan.h:803`): The managed-lane mode supplies context and preference. Traffic holds and route restrictions remain enforceable.
- **Prefer lane following where suitable** (action, `energyplan.h:836`): On managed lanes all moving candidates remain eligible. A goal or held scene retains its prior mode rules.
- **Consider offset and steering alternatives** (action, `energyplan.h:867`): Avoid can refine the best rollouts when refinement is enabled. It uses the same tier comparison for managed lanes.
- **Score retreat alongside normal driving** (action, `recovery.h:186`): The measured breadcrumb return path is an ordinary candidate. A clearer forward or turning move can win over a preferred retrace.
- **An enforced hold or unavailable command can brake** (state, `recovery.h:214`): A managed lane without a Traffic hold uses the ranked motion set. When no permitted command exists, the previous brake fallback remains.
- **Rank clearance first and preference second** (decision, `energyplan.h:922`): A better clearance tier releases the incumbent immediately. Existing commitment timing applies within the same tier.
- **Publish this slot’s timed trajectory** (state, `planenergy.h:468`): The worker publishes the chosen path, hover mode, speed, timestamp, vehicle and lane/goal kind. An exception logs and releases the busy guard. In eligible forward, on-wheels Follow, Plan publishes a stable lane-centre steering reference for the wheel follower. The reference uses the same scene to check sampled static clearance before switching. Radar and other trajectory consumers continue to use the collision-scored rollout. The wheel follower uses the rollout when the lane reference is ineligible. When a verified point left the snapshot and the full-speed centre candidate is feasible, Recover's Stand returns to Follow at once (verified_floor_retry) and the wheel command is held to 1.25 m/s for 0.75 s. Grade uses nine samples and a two-pass fit. A stationary candidate may hold its starting position, and a car already above its grade limit may move toward a gentler grade. A sustained climb above the existing grade limit remains infeasible. plan.choices reports raw and fitted starting grade, sample counts, and corrections.
  - in PlanWork: PlanWork on the thread pool, one memory per slot: Memory and field reset when another car takes the slot or it switches between lane and goal scenes. Goal jobs go to PlanGoal. When a verified point left the snapshot and the full-speed centre candidate is feasible, Recover's Stand returns to Follow at once (verified_floor_retry) and the wheel command is held to 1.25 m/s for 0.75 s. Grade uses nine samples and a two-pass fit. A stationary candidate may hold its starting position, and a car already above its grade limit may move toward a gentler grade. A sustained climb above the existing grade limit remains infeasible. plan.choices reports raw and fitted starting grade, sample counts, and corrections. World records recent successful, unsaturated central hull-sweep spans that contain no retained blocking contact. The sweep can follow the published reverse rollout as well as the forward rollout. Remembered sweep evidence helps select a checkpoint and carries no clearance authority over the current scene. · log `OBS system=plan.energy`
- **Fresh feasible result for this car and scene?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:5842`): The reader requires the correct vehicle and lane/goal kind, success and age of at most 500 ms. Otherwise command zero speed and brake.
- **Track wheel path or hover trajectory** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:5846`): Wheel path uses look-ahead and FollowSpeed. Hover uses the trajectory follower, including alignment and landing. Later control stages publish the applicable channels. In eligible forward, on-wheels Follow, Plan publishes a stable lane-centre steering reference for the wheel follower. The reference uses the same scene to check sampled static clearance before switching. Radar and other trajectory consumers continue to use the collision-scored rollout. The wheel follower uses the rollout when the lane reference is ineligible. For forward lane-centre Follow on native tyres, the wheel follower uses fresh yaw from the same vehicle. The correction projects heading during steering unwind, based on the previous command and existing slew limit. Measured yaw is compared with the lane turn rate so steady curve tracking keeps its feed-forward turn.
- **Brake while a command is unavailable** (state, `vehicleflight.cpp:5866`): A missing, stale or unavailable Plan result retains the existing brake fallback.
- **Hover planner reads the craft's flight row (FC-1)** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:2613` · log `OBS system=plan.flightspec`): FlightSpec sets bicycle::Spec hoverYaw = hull yaw, hoverLat = hull lateral (hulllogic::Flight with the row's turn share) and hoverTop = min(frame top, row top) before each lane (vehicleflight.cpp:5609) and goal (vehicleflight.cpp:4625) submit. The planner's hover turn is HoverYawAt = min(hoverYaw, hoverLat / max(4, v)) (bicycle.h:27), used by HoverStep (bicycle.h:62) and the hover rollouts (energyplan.h:553); the fixed 1.6 rad/s is gone. NoteFlightPlan (vehicleflight.cpp:2425) records the lane's wanted speed and the committed hover path's PeakTurn for FG-1.
- **GR-2: grade profile repair and start grade fit** (action, `planinput.h:20` · log `OBS system=plan.choices`): RepairGradeStart replaces a suspect leading floor sample only in the grade profile (scene.gradeFloor, planinput.h:152) when three contiguous forward measurements agree on a local slope and the extrapolated height fits the measured car base; collision and low-edge checks keep the measured floor. energyplan::FitGrade (energyplan.h:252) fits nine samples along the car in two passes, setting outliers aside. In Score a candidate holding its start (holdingStart) and a car already over its limit moving to a gentler grade (retreatingToGentlerGrade) are not rejected as too steep (energyplan.h:419); a sustained climb over carcap MaxGrade stays infeasible. plan.choices logs raw_start_grade, start_grade, grade_points, grade_set_aside, max_grade and floor_set_aside.
  - in RepairGradeStart: GR-2: repair a suspect starting sample in the grade profile: Three contiguous forward measurements agreeing on a local slope, with the extrapolated height consistent with the car base, replace a suspect leading sample in scene.gradeFloor only; collision and low-edge checks keep the measured floor. Samples set aside are counted (floorSetAside).
- **Wheel-clearance vote on each rollout (865DFD5C)** (action, `energyplan.h:514` · log `OBS system=plan.choices wheel_vote_speed= wheel_vote_reason=`): Inside Score, the first wheel command of every ordinary rollout is swept by wheelguard::Check (wheelguard.h:32) on the same World snapshot to its stopping distance. Its suggested speed and reason join the score (collision cost 12 x deficit / demand) and, on managed lanes, a clearance tier (10 when the suggestion is under 0.1 m/s). WG-2: the 0.15 m margin and the 1.25 m/s crawl on an unverified scene apply only to a constrained move (clearance under 0.65 m, not following the lane, or an offset). The Arbiter chooses; the Controller publishes with no separate clearance veto or pedal rewrite (GP-1's post-controller cap is gone).
  - in Score: Score movement and recovery, including clearance tiers: Managed lanes score ordinary and recovery motion in one candidate set. Crawl, steering, reverse, and an available breadcrumb retrace compete. Static and moving contact, predicted grade, and terminal braking room raise the clearance tier. A 100-point tier step dominates the bounded preference score. Equal tiers use the original motion costs and recovery preferences. A better tier releases commitment immediately. Explicit Traffic holds, route stop lines, and travel or capability restrictions keep their enforcement. Goal scenes and health pulses retain their existing rules. The short wheel-clearance check evaluates the first wheel command of each ordinary rollout on the same World snapshot. Its suggested speed and reason contribute to Plan's score, including a clearance tier on managed lanes. A zero suggestion is eligible for selection. The Arbiter owns the choice and the Controller publishes its result. A rollout whose body enters a known band or median is refused by the same collision rule as any wall, with reject reason enters_a_known_band (a sensed wall is enters_a_solid); a car already touching a band alone may take a path that does not carry its centre deeper (kNoGoTolerance 0.03 m, kNoGoRise 0.05 m); trace plan.nogo. · log `OBS system=plan.choices`
  - in Score: GR-2: start grade fit; may this candidate hold or ease off a steep start?: FitGrade: nine samples over the car's length, two-pass fit with outliers set aside. A candidate over carcap MaxGrade is rejected unless it holds its start (under 0.1 m/s, within 0.1 m) or the car already starts over the limit and the candidate moves to a grade at least 0.02 gentler; a sustained climb over the limit stays infeasible. On managed lanes the excess grade raises the clearance tier. · log `OBS system=plan.choices raw_start_grade start_grade grade_points grade_set_aside max_grade floor_set_aside`
- **Known legal intervals from the road lanes and bands** (action, `planinput.h:101` · log `OBS system=plan.nogo`): KnownLegal (planinput.h:101) turns frame.lanes (planrules.h planbranch::Lanes: left/right per lane plus bandLow / bandHigh) into the lateral intervals, in the route line's frame (+ right), where a body may stand. Lanes that touch merge; a gap between lanes is a median and stays a gap; every listed lane counts, open or not (a closed lane is the coordinator's rule, not a band). An outer end is a finite hard edge only on a side that reports a band; with no band and no gap nothing is known and the list is empty, so older roads and older addons behave exactly as before. planinput::Scene stores the result as Scene::legal (planinput.h:128). It is known from the road, never sensed, so it does not flicker. Build described: core DCDD57CB + Traffic BAC5B692 (source and offline checks only; in-game proof pending).
- **Does a wheels rollout's body enter a band or median?** (decision, `energyplan.h:331` · log `OBS system=plan.nogo`): Score refuses a wheels rollout whose body enters a no-go band or median (planscene.h Scene::NoGoDepth, line 246: how far the body at its lateral offset and heading to the route is past a finite end of the interval that holds its centre; the body reach is half its width turned to the road plus half its length across it; with a finite end on both sides a road narrower than the body is judged from its middle, so a wide car is not 'in' a band just by standing centred). Reject reason enters_a_known_band; tolerance kNoGoTolerance 0.03 m (energyplan.h:39), rise allowance kNoGoRise 0.05 m. A car already inside a band may leave or hold but not go deeper (the same 'way out' rule as a touching car). Only the planner decides this, on the Scene's own legal list; no band reaches the Controller as a veto. Hover rollouts and goal scenes without lanes have no legal list and are unchanged. Trace: OBS system=plan.nogo (planenergy.h, diagnostic build) event=body_in_known_no_go / body_clear_of_known_no_go with depth, lateral, refused_candidates, intervals, reason. Names (reject reason enters_a_known_band, kNoGoRise 0.05) are as reported by the change owner; at atlas time the source grep showed kNoGoTolerance 0.03 (energyplan.h:39) and the older reject label enters_a_known_band, so confirm names when the change lands. Build described: core DCDD57CB + Traffic BAC5B692 (source and offline checks only; in-game proof pending).

Arrows: snapshot → queue (Submit); queue → scene (Accepted); queue → latest (Read previous result); scene → corridor (Optional health request); test → result (Pulse safe); test → candidates (No pulse / unsafe pulse); candidates → mode (Feasibility and progress); mode → follow (Follow); mode → avoid (Avoid); mode → recover (Recover); mode → stand (Stand); follow → choose (Finite candidate); avoid → choose (Finite candidate); recover → choose (Finite candidate); follow → stand (No finite candidate); avoid → stand (No finite candidate); recover → stand (No finite candidate); choose → result (Keep or switch); stand → result (Zero-speed result); result → latest (Next frame reader); latest → track (Yes); latest → brake (No); corridor → test (Shifted lane line); flightspec → snapshot (Hover spec in the frame); scene → graderepair (Lane floor); graderepair → candidates (Start grade and grade profile); candidates → wheelvote (First wheel command); wheelvote → choose (Suggested speed, reason, tier); scene → known (frame.lanes with bandLow / bandHigh); known → corridor (Scene::legal (known edges)); known → candidates (Scene::legal (NoGoDepth)); candidates → nogo (Each wheels rollout vs Scene::legal); nogo → choose (Refused rollouts leave the ranking; leaving or holding stays allowed)

## Wheel and hover actuation  `#actuation`  (Movement and flight)

The frame loop decides control channels. Native physics runs through the bound chassis and actuator, including CF-1 on eligible wheel steps.

- **Active vehicle frame** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:8918`): Refresh vehicle world state and environment, tick the player and NPC pilots, then maintain body bindings.
- **Autopilot running?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:6121`): Autopilot supplies energy-plan mode, bearing, target speed and braking. Manual controls enter through the applicable input path. Goal arrival or timeout can end autopilot.
- **Drive or hover actuator?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:6391`): A valid occupied drive transition stops the hover writer below the vertical-speed limit. LeaveDrive restores hover for an authorised mode change or other exit. On a wheel-entering start Auto can stay on its wheels.
  - in Fly: Autopilot: wheels wanted (script travel or plan mode Drive)?: A scripted/manual kTravel command (1 float, 2 drive) wins, else adOut.mode. Drive wanted but empty vehicle or not yet down: groundHug (hover held low). Enter on the game's wheels only when occupied, ground known, < 2.5 m above, |vz| < 4. Leave when the plan wants hover (LeaveDrive).
  - in Fly: Drive mode, autopilot ended, it started on wheels?: Yes: Stop("autopilot ended: back on its wheels"): the car goes back to the player's wheels, no hover. · log `vehicle.handover`
  - in Fly: Drive mode with no autopilot? Leave drive (hover on): Never keep overriding the controls: LeaveDrive("autopilot ended").
- **Follow path and steer on wheels** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:6477`): An eligible rail tyres follower tracks the plan (tyre controller railtyres::StepSigned: SP-1 grade push = clamp(grade x 2.0, 0, 0.8) uphill from g_roadGrade, TM-1 held throttle kept per direction across stops, fading over 6 s; Cruise paid boost raises the rail speed to min(speed x boost multiplier, corner cap) while only top speed limits) and publishes tracking error for the next speed decision. For non-manual output, steering is capped by carcap TyreLat(gear grip) (ST-1) and rate-limited before publication. In eligible forward, on-wheels Follow, Plan publishes a stable lane-centre steering reference for the wheel follower. The reference uses the same scene to check sampled static clearance before switching. Radar and other trajectory consumers continue to use the collision-scored rollout. The wheel follower uses the rollout when the lane reference is ineligible. For forward lane-centre Follow on native tyres, the wheel follower uses fresh yaw from the same vehicle. The correction projects heading during steering unwind, based on the previous command and existing slew limit. Measured yaw is compared with the lane turn rate so steady curve tracking keeps its feed-forward turn. After the native tyre step, slow grounded rail-tyre motion can receive a bounded extra impulse in the requested direction along the road plane. This applies during forward motion and reverse recovery. Traffic holds retain priority. The wheel-clearance check now runs inside Plan for each rollout (865DFD5C); the Controller adds no clearance veto after it. Route edge no-go bands are not an actuation rule. The planner shifts the followed line off known edges (corridor) and refuses rollouts whose body enters a band or median (Score, enters_a_known_band), so this follower tracks the chosen path or lane-centre reference as before; the Controller adds no band veto and no pedal or steering rewrite for them (one arbiter, one Controller). Older roads without bands behave exactly as before. Build described: core DCDD57CB + Traffic BAC5B692 (source and offline checks only; in-game proof pending). BO-1: the game's forward boost (input block +0x1C) is executed from Output::boost, Speed's request; only on tyres (velocity-write rail and hover never boost). REPLACED: the old o.speed > 30 boost in the wheel stage. The actuator measures the tyre pedal's unclamped demand (railtyres Signed.demand) and publishes it for Speed; the Controller adds no boost decision and no boost veto. BO-1 is included in diagnostic core 53D2FDBC. In-game verification is pending.
- **Resolve the wheel command owner at handoff** (decision, `npcvehicles.cpp:6731`): Vehicle Coordinator handoff admits the command owner before planning. Revocation on takeover or world reset invalidates old grants. The mailbox transports an admitted command and checks freshness without reading Traffic policy. npcvehicles::AdmitWheelOutput (called from Fly, vehicleflight.cpp:5936) admits only the pilot's own cold-start Traffic job (task 9) for that vehicle and captures the current handoff generation (trafficpool::CaptureWheelHandoff, api_traffic.cpp:1261; 0 = refused when the player took it). Other pilots publish without a revocable grant (generation 0).
- **Publish wheel command or clear it** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:6645`): The mailbox carries steer, accelerator, brake and boost. Native NPC control consumers read the vehicle-specific mailbox. The Controller translates the selected motion into wheel or rail commands. It performs no separate wheel-clearance veto or clearance-based pedal rewrite. Explicit holds, normal speed control and braking remain available. All pilots use PublishAdmitted for wheel commands. The wheel trace reports selected speed, actual speed and actuator values. Route policy and sensing have their own traces. All pilots publish through WheelMailbox::PublishAdmitted (handoff.h:68): an allowed admission whose generation is still current publishes; a refused or stale one clears the mailbox.
- **Publish hover controls** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:6841`): The shared hover controller receives its active state and target channels. The physics model uses its matched body, measured orientation and configured flight tuning. FC-1: with the hover channels the flight gear row (g_sh.row) and the gear-scaled hull (g_sh.hull) are published for the step's writers.
- **Resolve a unique body owner** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:1778`): The step hook sees every chassis and matches a pilot through the body binding. A duplicate claim is marked ambiguous. Unowned chassis run the original step without inheriting player controls.
  - in ApplyGrip: BEFORE the wheels: tyre grip and turning weight x gear (GT-1): Per vehicle data block: front friction x gearN%, rear x gearN% x rearN%, yaw inverse inertia / weightN%. Defaults gear1 160/130/250, gear2 125/120/175, gear3 game's own, gear4 100/115/150. GO-1 (2026-11-19): the block is the car's own (one per instance); wheelgrip::Want decides one owner if a block is ever shared (player slot 0 owns it; AI cars do not change it while that car is fresh; else the highest ask). A block address reused by another car is read again (block_reused). Failed writes never retried. · log `vehicle.grip owner= rule=`
- **Run the native vehicle step** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:1793`): Apply grip and eligible traction before the original step (GO-1: ApplyGrip writes the car's own data block; wheelgrip::Want picks one owner if a block were shared, and a reused block address is read again; log vehicle.grip owner= rule=), then record slip. Manual and autopilot wheels share the native step.
- **Eligible CF-1 wheel step?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:1796`): Requires a resolved grip slot, no active hover or direct rail, readable chassis data, the proper wheel contact and upright state, nonzero gear budget and speed above the floor.
- **Apply four-corner correction** (action, `cornerforce.h:66`): Learn steering sign, cap desired yaw by carcap TyreLat(Input::grip) / speed, then target yaw tracking and rear slide. Allocate side force and yaw moment across four corners, cap per gear, smooth and apply side velocity and spin changes.
- **Run applicable bound post-step model** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:2001`): A bound owner hands off to StepModel. It can apply the active hover controller or rail branch under validity and expiry checks. The no-owner path returns the native result after limited passive observation. After the native tyre step, slow grounded rail-tyre motion can receive a bounded extra impulse in the requested direction along the road plane. This applies during forward motion and reverse recovery. Traffic holds retain priority. The wheel-clearance check now runs inside Plan for each rollout (865DFD5C); the Controller adds no clearance veto after it.
- **Record step and tracking evidence** (state, `vehicleflight.cpp:1653`): Observe `vehicle.corners`, `drive.tracking`, and native handoff physics evidence to diagnose behaviour by vehicle and pilot.
  - in MeasureRear: AFTER: measure rear slip angle and yaw flips (RB-1): Bicycle-model rear slip angle and yaw rate into g_rearSlipDeg / g_yawMeasured (read by auto gear and tracking). Upright (up.z >= 0.5) only. · log `vehicle.grip`
- **Native reader: mailbox fresh and grant current?** (decision, `handoff.h:80`): DriveFor (vehicleflight.cpp:8138) reads each pilot's WheelMailbox at the input hook: the vehicle must match, the version must be even and unchanged across the read (two attempts, no lock), and handoff::WheelGrantCurrent(generation) must hold (0 = not revocable). The mailbox carries an admitted command only; it reads no Traffic policy.
  - in Read: Input hook: mailbox fresh for this vehicle and grant current?: vehicleflight::DriveFor reads each pilot's mailbox: same vehicle, an even version unchanged across the read (two attempts, writers serialized, reader lock-free), and a generation still current (0 = not revocable). Takeover and world reset bump the generation (D.taken, D.wheelReset). No Traffic policy is read here.
- **Revoke wheel grants on takeover or world reset** (action, `api_traffic.cpp:618` · log `OBS system=traffic.taken event=player_took`): NoteTaken (player took the vehicle) and the traffic service Reset (api_traffic.cpp:1243, world reset / load) increment handoff::wheelGeneration, so every earlier grant fails WheelGrantCurrent and its mailbox command is ignored.
- **Wheel trace: selected speed, actual speed, actuators** (state, `vehicleflight.cpp:6551` · log `OBS system=drive.wheel`): Every 0.5 s on a rail-driven car: selected_speed (the plan's), rail_target, fwd (actual forward speed), reverse, accel_before_rail, brake_before_rail, aim error and yaw command; event=no_rail_step when the rail's physics step has not run for 300 ms.

Arrows: author → choose (Plan or manual command); choose → wheel (Wheels); choose → hover (Hover); publish → body (Subsequent physics tick); hover → body (Subsequent physics tick); body → stock (Matched or unowned); stock → assist (After game step); assist → corners (Eligible); assist → post (Skip correction); corners → post (Post-wheel correction); post → done (Result); frame → authority (Vehicle owner); authority → author (Owner grant accompanies the command); wheel → publish (Publish the admitted command); publish → mailbox (Input hook reads (DriveFor)); revoke → mailbox (Generation bumped: old grants fail); wheel → wheeltrace (Rail drive, every 0.5 s)

## Manual and AI hover flight  `#hover`  (Movement and flight)

Live vehicle hover runs in Fly and the bound chassis step. The physics step chooses velocity/spin control or the optional native impulse model. FC-1 / FG-1 (2026-11-19): one per-gear flight row drives both writers and the hover planner; the autopilot picks its flight gear by FG-1.

- **Process active flight** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:5936`): Read the current chassis, flight state, vehicle and pilot role, route policy and physics observations.
- **Manual or pilot-controlled?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:6155`): Manual control uses focused key/analog input. Autopilot reads the energy planner; direct held pilot commands can override their specified channels.
- **Read throttle, aim, roll and lift** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:6212`): Throttle and brake drive forward thrust and slowing; throttle lock/paid boost modify it. Camera aim supplies pitch and heading. Turn inputs produce roll, lift keys or Alt drop adjust vertical input.
- **Track planned bearing, speed and height** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:6736`): Follow the hover trajectory and requested bearing; strafe separates nose and travel when required. Scripted direct commands get a final wall/person/vehicle envelope.
- **Choose vertical command or height hold** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:6666`): Use ground and allowable corridor measurements as the height reference. Liftoff holds short hover height. Manual hover holds when level, releases on pitch or lift. AI can use lane/goal and clearance height.
- **Landing conditions satisfied?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:6802`): AI LandOnArrival needs a close hold target and fresh safe site proof, then limits vertical correction and brakes until a safe slow touchdown. Manual/ordinary settling stops after the low, descending, no-thrust conditions persist.
- **End hover and release controller** (state, `vehicleflight.cpp:3562`): Stop clears active controls on landed or supported touchdown. An AI landing who has not touched down safely keeps its bounded descent command.
- **Publish hover channels and camera attitude** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:6838`): Copy thrust, brake, yaw/roll/pitch aim, lift/strafe, hold, cap and ground evidence into the shared pilot state. Physical flight publishes measured pitch and roll to the vehicle camera.
- **Valid body and physical VTOL active?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:2169`): After the native game step, the matching active body publishes orientation. Opt-in Physical VTOL/Physical AI also needs ready hooks and no chassis fault.
  - in StepDetour: Hooked native vehicle step (every vehicle, every physics step): Installed on addr::kVehicleStep by vehicleflight::Install. Counts steps, notes the body, observes chassis mass (bodymass).
- **Run stock velocity and spin controller** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:2349`): Rotate about the active body axes or stock yaw/bank. Integrate nose thrust, horizontal braking/drag, strafe, climb and gravity trim, cap speed and retain measured attitude. FC-1: the written spin eases to the wanted rates at the gear row's yaw / tilt response (flightgear::Ease, vehicleflight.cpp:2144) and the speed across the nose leaks at the row's side gain past its deadband (flightgear::SideLeak, vehicleflight.cpp:2187); gear 3 = 4 /s, 5 /s, 1.2 /s as before.
- **Find physical attitude and vertical rates** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:1330`): AI uses vtolai::Rates for yaw and bank. Assisted manual turns coordinate camera aim and bank; free control measures aim errors in the body basis. Limit vertical targets and apply known ground clearance.
  - in PreparePhysical: Physical requested and motion readable? Own the chassis: Stores physInstance/physBody/physChassis; any other slot holding this instance releases it.
- **Solve bounded impulse pair** (action, `vtolphysics.h:80`): Validate body axes, inertia, mass and timestep. Collective uses vertical trim with anti-windup; forward and side thrust are bounded. Convert angular control to inertia-scaled torque impulse. FC-1: rate gain, angular limit, side gain, side budget and deadband come from the gear row (StepPhysical fills vtol::Command from s.row, vehicleflight.cpp:1367). StableRateGain = min(gain, max(5, 0.8 / dt)) keeps a high low-gear gain stable at long steps; gear 3 and below are unchanged. The side push acts along the body's right, so when banked its small vertical share stays and the lift controller holds height.
  - in Solve: Physical VTOL: rates -> vtol::Solve -> native impulses: Rates from vtolai (autopilot), vtolassist (assist, roll < 60 deg) or freeroll aim. Impulses through write vt+0xC0/+0xC8 (checked addresses). Parked wipe 5 steps or a fault -> stock model for that vehicle. FC-1: vtol::Command takes rateGain, angularLimit, sideGain, sideBudget and the gear-4 deadband from the gear row (s.row); Solve bounds the rate gain with StableRateGain = min(gain, max(5, 0.8 / dt)); gear 3 = the old numbers.
- **Apply valid physical output?** (decision, `vehicleflight.cpp:1372`): The guarded actuator submits linear/angular impulses and then rereads spin. Invalid solves leave the native result. Failed native writes or repeated AI speed wipes fault the physical chassis and release its ownership.
- **Keep native result or resume stock hover** (state, `vehicleflight.cpp:1382`): A physical apply fault releases physical ownership, so future matching steps can use standard hover. An unbound or invalid body leaves the engine’s result.
- **Record actual velocity, attitude and step** (state, `vehicleflight.cpp:1383`): The active matched body updates feedback and timing for the next frame. The physical camera and planning consumers read that feedback.
- **Flight gear row (FC-1): one handling row per gear** (state, `carcap.h:51`): carcap::Flight(gear, ai) returns the one FlightRow that both hover writers, the hull's turn limit and the hover planner read. Physical VTOL: rate gain 10/7/5/3 /s, angular limit 12/9/6/4 rad/s^2, drift (side) gain 3/2/1.2/0.5 /s within a side budget of 12/8/3/2 m/s^2 for the player (AI 12/12/6/4); a side deadband only in gear 4 (0.5 + 0.05 x speed). Standard hover: yaw response 8/5.6/4/2.4 /s, tilt 10/7/5/3 /s, side leak = the same side gain. Both: turn strength (lateralScale) 1.33/1.11/1/0.67 of the hull's sustained turn and top kFlightTop 30/60/100/150 m/s. Gear 3 is the pre-change flight bit for bit (tests/flightgear_test.cpp). carcap::TurnAt(yaw, lateral, v) = min(yaw, lateral / max(4, |v|)) is the one turn formula (hulllogic::Limits::YawAt, bicycle::Spec::HoverYawAt). Lift is not in the row.
- **Autopilot flight gear (FG-1): which gear now?** (decision, `flightgear.h:56` · log `OBS system=vehicle.gear event=flight_shift`): flightgear::Target, called from Fly (vehicleflight.cpp:6053): gear 1 for landing, take-off (lift-off or still on wheels), strafe, a plan under 1 m/s, under 3 m over the floor, within 15 m of the stop or within 20 m of a wall or vehicle ahead; otherwise the lowest gear whose kFlightTop reaches the route's wanted speed. If the committed hover plan's sharpest turn (PeakTurn x max(4, speed)) needs more lateral than that gear's hull holds, the highest lower gear that holds it; one gear up on a straight leg over 200 m from the stop. flightgear::Update holds: down after 0.4 s, up one gear after 2.5 s (AG-1's holds). The gear's top caps the autopilot's speed. The player's flight gear is the gear key's (no automatic shift); AG-1 stays the rule on wheels.
  - in Target: FG-1: autopilot flight gear from landing, obstacles, speed and turns: Gear 1 for landing, take-off, strafe, plan under 1 m/s, under 3 m over the floor, within 15 m of the stop or 20 m of a wall or vehicle; else the lowest gear whose top reaches the wanted speed, lower if the committed plan's sharpest turn needs more lateral than the gear holds, one up on a straight leg over 200 m. Down after 0.4 s, up one gear after 2.5 s. Player: the gear key's gear. On wheels the flight gear restarts; AG-1 is the ground rule. The plan's sharpest turn comes from flightgear::PeakTurn over the committed hover path. · log `OBS system=vehicle.gear event=flight_shift`
- **Apply the gear row: hull turn share and top** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:6259` · log `OBS system=vehicle.flightgear event=row / event=track`): Fly takes carcap::Flight(flightGear, autopilot). The hover publish stores it in g_sh.row and sets g_sh.hull = hulllogic::Flight(hullBase, physical && !fault, row.lateralScale) (vehicleflight.cpp:6685; physical caps lateral at 9 m/s^2 and brakes at min(brake, 2 x speed)). Every planner reads g_sh.hull; the physics step reads g_sh.row. vehicle.flightgear event=row logs the row on a gear, role or writer change; event=track logs each second while hovering (rate error, drift, side push, angular / torque / side capped). The player's gear key logs vehicle.gear event=flight_shift reason=player_gear_key and shows the row in its notice.

Arrows: request → pilot (Active frame); pilot → manual (Manual); pilot → auto (AI / held pilot command); manual → altitude (Control axes); auto → altitude (Plan axes); altitude → land (Check support); land → stop (Settled, supported or timed); land → publish (Still airborne / descending); publish → physics (Next bound vehicle step); physics → native (Valid standard model); physics → rates (Valid physical model); physics → fallback (No valid body); native → feedback (Velocity and spin); rates → solve (Rates and thrust); solve → apply (Validate / submit); apply → feedback (Applied); apply → fallback (Invalid or fault); feedback → request (Next frame); pilot → fg1 (Autopilot: flight gear); fg1 → rowapply (Held gear); manual → rowapply (Player: gear key gear); row → rowapply (carcap::Flight(gear, ai)); rowapply → publish (Row and hull into Shared); row → solve (Rate, angle and side gains); row → native (Yaw / tilt response, side leak)

## Standalone flight model and tuning  `#flight-math`  (Movement and flight)

This pure-math model and tuning code are listed in the core build. FVO loads saved tuning at startup. The current production source has no consumer of fm::Model and no call to flighttune::Apply or flighttune::Tick. The live hover graph shows the active rover actuator.

- **Reset flight model** (action, `flightmodel.cpp:31`): Set position, yaw, speed and neutral rates, then build the first input requests.
- **Sample and feed terrain** (action, `flightmodel.cpp:57`): Issue predicted hull and look-ahead height requests. Feed stores prior requests and answers for a later Step.
- **Mouse yaw scheme selected?** (decision, `flightmodel.cpp:95`): Mouse yaw mode uses pitch and yaw, optional filtered mouse-rate hysteresis, and cosmetic bank. The other scheme uses mouse pitch and roll with rudder yaw.
- **Yaw/pitch with cosmetic bank** (action, `flightmodel.cpp:99`): Apply smoothed mouse speed, suppress jitter, level pitch when inactive, then bank the hull according to yaw rate.
- **Virtual stick and rudder** (action, `flightmodel.cpp:120`): Use spring-back virtual sticks for pitch and roll, and speed-dependent rudder authority. A bank contributes to the yaw target.
- **Measure climb and stopping envelope** (action, `flightmodel.cpp:195`): Unknown or uncleared terrain limits approach speed by known stopping room and climb ability. Add vertical clearance assist; pitch assist is gated by the selected scheme and landing envelope.
- **Integrate attitude and throttle** (action, `flightmodel.cpp:278`): Ease pitch/yaw/roll rates. Simple speed moves toward the throttle target. The alternate speed branch also accounts for climb and turn losses. Predictive braking applies the terrain limit.
- **Enabled, low-speed stall away from ground?** (decision, `flightmodel.cpp:324`): If eligible, accumulate sink, lower the nose to its bound and start recovery. Otherwise decay sink; near ground caps it.
- **Integrate flight path and translation** (action, `flightmodel.cpp:341`): Align velocity toward the nose with lag. Combine along-path motion, strafe, lift, assist and sink to advance position.
- **Below a measured hull floor?** (decision, `flightmodel.cpp:387`): Clamp altitude and downward flight path if intersecting measured floor. Low-speed light contact marks Grounded, otherwise Scraping.
- **Publish state and next sample requests** (state, `flightmodel.cpp:408`): Select Grounded, Scraping, Assist, Stall, Warning or Cruise, then plan the next requests.
- **Load the model's tuning file** (action, `flighttune.cpp:108`): Startup calls Load. Parse matching keys in flight.ini, clamp numeric values and remember explicitly tuned entries. Missing file keeps unset defaults.
- **Tuning entry changed?** (decision, `flighttune.cpp:145`): The uncalled Tick path handles focused numpad input. Select/display, increment/decrement and clamp a value, or restore its model default.
- **Store set overrides** (action, `flighttune.cpp:79`): The tuning helper saves only explicit overrides. Apply can copy those overrides into a supplied fm::Params object, but the production FVO source does not invoke it.

Arrows: reset → measure (Initial requests); measure → axis (Advance one step); axis → easy (Yes); axis → bank (No); easy → clearance (Control targets); bank → clearance (Control targets); clearance → speed (Safe caps and lift); speed → stall (Check stall); stall → integrate (Stall forces); stall → integrate (Normal / recovery); integrate → floor (Check contact); floor → mode (Grounded / scraping); floor → mode (Above floor); mode → measure (Next step); load → edit (Available tuning code); edit → save (Change or reset); edit → edit (Select, show or idle)

## Cached ship hull preview  `#ship-model`  (Movement and flight)

ShipVehicle and ShipModel provide a visual rover preview and diagnostics for a cached ship exterior. The preview does not fly the player’s ship from its pilot seat.

- **Check current vehicle and ship seat** (action, `shipvehicle.cpp:48`): Detect a changed rover and clear the prior preview request. Periodically check the native pilot seat and report cached exterior availability.
