  - in Tick: ShipPreview (F10) triggered while in a vehicle?: Toggles the visual-only hull preview; shown only while vehicleflight::Active (hovering). Leaving or changing vehicle turns it off. Also checks every 500 ms whether the player sits in a ship pilot seat.
- **Preview requested on an active rover?** (decision, `shipvehicle.cpp:83`): The configured ShipPreview binding toggles visuals while the player has a vehicle. ShowOnVehicle gets a zero target without a wanted active FVO hover.
  - in Tick: ShipPreview (F10) triggered while in a vehicle?: Toggles the visual-only hull preview; shown only while vehicleflight::Active (hovering). Leaving or changing vehicle turns it off. Also checks every 500 ms whether the player sits in a ship pilot seat.
- **Restore or clear old visual** (action, `shipmodel.cpp:849`): Detach and restore the vehicle body when the old scene root is live; otherwise clear the stale attachment state.
  - in OnPlayerUpdate: Clone home ship 3D and show it on the driven vehicle: Runs in the game tick. NiCloningProcess clone stripped of collision, attached to the vehicle (or player) root; the vehicle's own visible parts hidden; real ship and chassis untouched. Load reset forgets everything.
  - in OnPlayerUpdate: No clone yet and 5 s since last try? capture the hull: Only with SP_FEATURE_VEHICLE; must happen while the landed exterior's modules are loaded (interior refuses). Missing 3D logs 'nothing to copy yet' and marks Unavailable when wanted.
- **Clone requested or capture due?** (decision, `shipmodel.cpp:866`): Capture can be explicit or periodic for a missing cache. Check whether this currently loaded home ship needs reconstruction.
  - in OnPlayerUpdate: Clone home ship 3D and show it on the driven vehicle: Runs in the game tick. NiCloningProcess clone stripped of collision, attached to the vehicle (or player) root; the vehicle's own visible parts hidden; real ship and chassis untouched. Load reset forgets everything.
  - in OnPlayerUpdate: No clone yet and 5 s since last try? capture the hull: Only with SP_FEATURE_VEHICLE; must happen while the landed exterior's modules are loaded (interior refuses). Missing 3D logs 'nothing to copy yet' and marks Unavailable when wanted.
- **Clone available exterior and strip collision** (action, `shipmodel.cpp:237`): Read the loaded exterior, assemble its visual clone and retain it across cell switches. If no loaded exterior is available, the preview can remain unavailable.
- **Requested, clone and target root valid?** (decision, `shipmodel.cpp:893`): Wait until a usable cached mesh and target scene root both exist.
  - in OnPlayerUpdate: Clone home ship 3D and show it on the driven vehicle: Runs in the game tick. NiCloningProcess clone stripped of collision, attached to the vehicle (or player) root; the vehicle's own visible parts hidden; real ship and chassis untouched. Load reset forgets everything.
  - in OnPlayerUpdate: No clone yet and 5 s since last try? capture the hull: Only with SP_FEATURE_VEHICLE; must happen while the landed exterior's modules are loaded (interior refuses). Missing 3D logs 'nothing to copy yet' and marks Unavailable when wanted.
- **Attach visual and place it on the target** (action, `shipmodel.cpp:896`): Restore a former target before switching, attach the clone to the current root, then place and orient it as a vehicle visual.
  - in OnPlayerUpdate: Clone home ship 3D and show it on the driven vehicle: Runs in the game tick. NiCloningProcess clone stripped of collision, attached to the vehicle (or player) root; the vehicle's own visible parts hidden; real ship and chassis untouched. Load reset forgets everything.
  - in OnPlayerUpdate: No clone yet and 5 s since last try? capture the hull: Only with SP_FEATURE_VEHICLE; must happen while the landed exterior's modules are loaded (interior refuses). Missing 3D logs 'nothing to copy yet' and marks Unavailable when wanted.
- **Leave the preview absent this frame** (state, `shipmodel.cpp:887`): A requested preview with no cached exterior is marked unavailable. The next update can retry when conditions change.
  - in OnPlayerUpdate: Clone home ship 3D and show it on the driven vehicle: Runs in the game tick. NiCloningProcess clone stripped of collision, attached to the vehicle (or player) root; the vehicle's own visible parts hidden; real ship and chassis untouched. Load reset forgets everything.
  - in OnPlayerUpdate: No clone yet and 5 s since last try? capture the hull: Only with SP_FEATURE_VEHICLE; must happen while the landed exterior's modules are loaded (interior refuses). Missing 3D logs 'nothing to copy yet' and marks Unavailable when wanted.
- **Optional diagnostic far probe** (action, `shipmodel.cpp:833`): The diagnostic-only branch updates the far-probe experiment independently of the regular preview attachment path.
  - in OnPlayerUpdate: Clone home ship 3D and show it on the driven vehicle: Runs in the game tick. NiCloningProcess clone stripped of collision, attached to the vehicle (or player) root; the vehicle's own visible parts hidden; real ship and chassis untouched. Load reset forgets everything.
  - in OnPlayerUpdate: No clone yet and 5 s since last try? capture the hull: Only with SP_FEATURE_VEHICLE; must happen while the landed exterior's modules are loaded (interior refuses). Missing 3D logs 'nothing to copy yet' and marks Unavailable when wanted.

Arrows: tick → toggle (Input/player update); tick → probe (Diagnostic build); toggle → cache (Wanted / background cache); toggle → detach (No preview); detach → cache (Allow capture check); cache → clone (Capture / replace); cache → ready (Reuse or wait); clone → ready (Check clone and root); ready → attach (Yes); ready → wait (No)

## NPC combat decisions and pilot authority  `#combat`  (NPC combat and damage)

Following jobs can enter combat, search, and pursue through the live NPC controller. Weapon authorization is a later gate.

- **Tick the following job** (action, `npcvehicles.cpp:3862`): Inspect occupancy, job phase, traffic/player ownership and wreck state. A wreck orders ejection and stops pilot and firing. An explicit task can take its own controller.
- **Can this job engage or search?** (decision, `npcvehicles.cpp:3920`): Require a valid scene, seated pilot and applicable order rules to select foes. Recall, cover, guard, rescue and explicit goals constrain aim, pursuit or fire.
- **Resolve combat and Weapons profiles** (action, `npcvehicles.cpp:3916`): Read the assigned profiles and effective Weapons restrictions. Enter an automatic combat profile derived from navigation when a foe appears, and clear it once combat has been over long enough.
- **Which motion branch is eligible?** (decision, `npcvehicles.cpp:4001`): Eligible chase can use the full AI, use the legacy manoeuvre path, or keep fixed defence/retreat goals. With no live foe, eligible full AI may search remembered contact.
- **Fuse visible, heard and remembered contact** (action, `combat_ai.h:122`): Observe target, velocity and health. Age contact memory; report changes, recent impacts, ally loss and approach evidence. Compute morale when enabled.
  - in Tick: Perception, behaviour tree, BFM, tactics, steering: Memory/impulses/morale (combat_brain.h, Halo-style tree, personality from faction, difficulty), BFM Pick with hysteresis (combat_bfm.h), Mad Max tactics (combat_tactics.h), Reynolds steering combined by priority with SafeAhead 2.5 s terrain check (combat_steer.h).
- **Select a combat action** (decision, `combat_ai.h:176`): The brain combines perceived contact, urgency, morale, health, personality and squad role with scored manoeuvres/tactics. Its action can hold, evade, retreat, execute a manoeuvre, search or support others.
  - in Tick: Perception, behaviour tree, BFM, tactics, steering: Memory/impulses/morale (combat_brain.h, Halo-style tree, personality from faction, difficulty), BFM Pick with hysteresis (combat_bfm.h), Mad Max tactics (combat_tactics.h), Reynolds steering combined by priority with SafeAhead 2.5 s terrain check (combat_steer.h).
- **Turn the action into a flight request** (action, `combat_ai.h:273`): Build a motion recipe, resolve desired direction and combine it with available avoidance. Publish a short navigation goal and optional direct control or aim request.
  - in Tick: Perception, behaviour tree, BFM, tactics, steering: Memory/impulses/morale (combat_brain.h, Halo-style tree, personality from faction, difficulty), BFM Pick with hysteresis (combat_bfm.h), Mad Max tactics (combat_tactics.h), Reynolds steering combined by priority with SafeAhead 2.5 s terrain check (combat_steer.h).
- **Search previous contact** (action, `npcvehicles.cpp:4018`): Re-enter the combat brain with no live target where chasing rules permit. A searching job keeps flying to the selected goal and clears active firing.
- **Apply legacy manoeuvre or positional defence** (action, `npcvehicles.cpp:4012`): A chase can use the manoeuvre path. Without an active manoeuvre, a combat position query can refine the goal for defence or retreat.
- **Is a combat line taking motion authority?** (decision, `npcvehicles.cpp:2442`): The plan can own motion and facing from the supplied aim point. A stun or a refused line leaves the fallback channel writer eligible.
  - in CombatAITick: Combat AI tick: carrot, sticks, aim, fire: Fills combatai::Situation, runs combatai::Tick, sets the goal carrot (hold or cruise speed). CB-2: when a combat line drives the plan only aim pitch goes as a stick (PilotCombatAim); stunned after a ram it drifts; otherwise steering commands. · log `plan.combat`
- **Provide goal and aim pitch to Plan** (action, `npcvehicles.cpp:2449`): Keep only optional pitch as a direct aim command while Plan moves the vehicle on its combat line.
  - in CombatAITick: Combat AI tick: carrot, sticks, aim, fire: Fills combatai::Situation, runs combatai::Tick, sets the goal carrot (hold or cruise speed). CB-2: when a combat line drives the plan only aim pitch goes as a stick (PilotCombatAim); stunned after a ram it drifts; otherwise steering commands. · log `plan.combat`
- **Provide held fallback pilot channels** (action, `npcvehicles.cpp:2453`): A stunned pilot drifts and spins. Otherwise eligible tactical controls can ask for yaw, throttle, strafe, vertical, gear and brake. A held position may request yaw and pitch only.
  - in CombatAITick: Combat AI tick: carrot, sticks, aim, fire: Fills combatai::Situation, runs combatai::Tick, sets the goal carrot (hold or cruise speed). CB-2: when a combat line drives the plan only aim pitch goes as a stick (PilotCombatAim); stunned after a ram it drifts; otherwise steering commands. · log `plan.combat`
- **Run flight or release the controller** (state, `npcvehicles.cpp:4025`): Controller selection gives jobs, explicit modes and rescue precedence. Losing the driver stops the pilot. Exit from manoeuvre clears stale AI commands.
- **Pass through to current job controller** (state, `npcvehicles.cpp:3875`): Wreck/eject, task, escort, rescue and explicit order branches keep their own lifecycle and motion decisions.

Arrows: tick → admit (Following and valid); tick → inactive (Wreck / explicit task); admit → profile (Eligible); admit → inactive (Blocked by state or order); profile → mode (Policy and foe); mode → memory (Full AI); mode → legacy (Legacy / positional); mode → search (No foe, search allowed); mode → inactive (Other controller wins); memory → choose (Updated blackboard); choose → build (Selected action); build → plan (Navigation and aim requests); plan → follow (Yes); plan → direct (No / stunned); follow → handoff (Planned movement); direct → handoff (Fallback controls); search → handoff (Search movement); legacy → handoff (Fallback goal)

## Weapon authorization and launch  `#weapons`  (NPC combat and damage)

NPC combat and unoccupied-rover Cover submit through the native vehicle input gate. Launch observations feed the damage and combat systems.

- **NPC aim and fire request** (action, `npcvehicles.cpp:4111`): Use the current final aim point, including AI aim where active; overwatch can turn the hull while navigation holds position.
- **Rover Cover or Guard fire request** (action, `vehicleflight.cpp:7083`): Unoccupied rover cover uses its own target, hold spot, aiming and Weapons profile. Its burst sends through FireVehicleWeapon.
- **Ready and clear to fire?** (decision, `npcvehicles.cpp:4127`): Require effective permission, weapon range, yaw and pitch alignment, clear line to target, and the AI's own permission where applicable.
- **Check game shot clearance** (action, `environment.cpp:360`): Start the ray beyond the shooter hull. A missing ray result refuses; an unobstructed result, target hit or obstruction at the end of the measured segment is accepted.
  - in EntrySite: Vehicle at rest on the ground for entry / exit?: Entry Safe once standing (sitelogic::Standing) or at <= 0.75 m/s on uneven ground; otherwise 'Vehicle must stop and descend before boarding'. BoardingSite also needs a usable boarding side.
- **Burst timer requests fire?** (decision, `npcvehicles.cpp:4140`): The burst state determines held fire and gaps. An ineligible shot cancels; clear the native firing latch if it had been set.
- **Hold or send release** (state, `npcvehicles.cpp:4150`): Send the native fire-off input if already firing. Diagnostic logs say profile/range, blocked shot, aim or AI safety.
- **Live chassis and player not driving it?** (decision, `npcvehicles.cpp:1206`): SubmitFire suppresses automatic firing for wrecked vehicles or where the player is driving.
- **Submit native vehicle fire input** (action, `npcvehicles.cpp:1230`): Send the fire bit with the currently published wheel channels for this chassis. Missing native input service or a guarded failure returns false.
- **Live automatic aim record available?** (decision, `vehicleaim.cpp:65`): The projectile launch hook checks the shooter against the current automatic aim record. Ordinary player shots retain native launch angles.
  - in Before: Turn the projectile at launch to the led aim: Projectile::Launch hook (FUN_141B3BD50): for an NPC job's vehicle the launch angles are set from npcvehicles::AimFor; player rover shots are only logged. Reports the handle to vehiclehits.
- **Set launch angles from stored aim** (action, `vehicleaim.cpp:83`): For a valid automatic aim point compute launch pitch and heading from projectile origin, then run native launch.
  - in Before: Turn the projectile at launch to the led aim: Projectile::Launch hook (FUN_141B3BD50): for an NPC job's vehicle the launch angles are set from npcvehicles::AimFor; player rover shots are only logged. Reports the handle to vehiclehits.
- **Observe completed native launch** (state, `vehicleaim.cpp:114`): Forward the native result. Record projectile handle, shooter and launch evidence for vehicle damage tracking and combat observations.

Arrows: npc → ray (Final aim point); cover → ray (Cover aim point); ray → allow (Clearance result); allow → burst (Allowed); allow → off (Refused); burst → gate (Start / continue); burst → off (Gap / release); gate → submit (Grant); gate → off (Suppress fire); submit → launch (Native projectile); launch → aim (Automatic); launch → observe (Native aim); aim → observe (Override aim)

## Vehicle damage, wrecking and repair  `#damage`  (NPC combat and damage)

FVO tracks chassis health and combat observations from projectile and other mod damage sources, with a common friendly-fire and feature gate.

- **Drain native impacts and launches** (action, `vehiclehits.cpp:561`): Update settings and projectile tracking. Use the current world snapshot including held vehicles for impact attribution.
  - in Tick: Follow shots and native impacts into vehicle hulls: Vehicles take no game damage, so launched projectiles (NoteLaunch from the launch hook) and native impacts (ImpactDetour) are tested against each listed vehicle's oriented hull (interactionlogic::HullHit); the shooter's own vehicle never counts. Also drives the enemy health bar (EnemyBarTick). · log `health`
- **Native impact or tracked fallback?** (decision, `vehiclehits.cpp:581`): Native impact tests the point against rewound vehicle hulls and chooses the nearest eligible chassis. Tracked launches support positional/sweep fallback and observed projectile ballistics.
  - in Tick: Follow shots and native impacts into vehicle hulls: Vehicles take no game damage, so launched projectiles (NoteLaunch from the launch hook) and native impacts (ImpactDetour) are tested against each listed vehicle's oriented hull (interactionlogic::HullHit); the shooter's own vehicle never counts. Also drives the enemy health bar (EnemyBarTick). · log `health`
- **Test tracked projectile segment** (action, `vehiclehits.cpp:622`): Project a disappeared, never-observed projectile where there is a launch line; otherwise use available positions. Keep tracking live shots and expire concluded ones.
  - in Tick: Follow shots and native impacts into vehicle hulls: Vehicles take no game damage, so launched projectiles (NoteLaunch from the launch hook) and native impacts (ImpactDetour) are tested against each listed vehicle's oriented hull (interactionlogic::HullHit); the shooter's own vehicle never counts. Also drives the enemy health bar (EnemyBarTick). · log `health`
- **Eligible distinct hostile hit?** (decision, `vehiclehits.cpp:495`): Exclude shooter and own chassis during attribution. Friendly candidates do not cause damage. Claim a projectile/vehicle pair once. A qualified hostile impact can provoke aggression independently of health settings.
- **Resolve native or fallback shot damage** (action, `vehiclehits.cpp:499`): Use native weapon damage when available, multiply falloff, apply native difficulty scaling for player involvement, then configured scale/fallback and single-shot cap.
- **Other vehicle damage request** (action, `vehiclehits.cpp:974`): Other runtime paths can submit chassis damage. Combat rams supply damage for the participating vehicles via this interface.
- **Vehicle damage enabled, finite and unfriendly?** (decision, `vehiclehits.cpp:460`): The common damage gate checks the feature, rejects invalid/nonpositive values, and rejects friendly sources.
  - in AddDamage: Add vehicle damage and the driver's share: Damage per hit = native weapon total x player difficulty x [hits] weaponScale (weapondamage.h: a native zero stays zero; [hits] damage=20 only when unreadable). Health 300 by default ([hits] health / limit). DriverShare passes part to the driver; healthlogic heals after a quiet interval that starts after combat. · log `health`
- **Accumulate damage and update health readers** (action, `vehiclehits.cpp:466`): Cap at the chassis limit, update attacker/time/hit records and distribute eligible driver damage. HUD and pilot readers use the resulting health fraction.
  - in AddDamage: Add vehicle damage and the driver's share: Damage per hit = native weapon total x player difficulty x [hits] weaponScale (weapondamage.h: a native zero stays zero; [hits] damage=20 only when unreadable). Health 300 by default ([hits] health / limit). DriverShare passes part to the driver; healthlogic heals after a quiet interval that starts after combat. · log `health`
- **Newly reached wreck limit?** (decision, `vehiclehits.cpp:480`): Only the first crossing enters wreck state. Existing damage updates feed the repair clock.
  - in AddDamage: Add vehicle damage and the driver's share: Damage per hit = native weapon total x player difficulty x [hits] weaponScale (weapondamage.h: a native zero stays zero; [hits] damage=20 only when unreadable). Health 300 by default ([hits] health / limit). DriverShare passes part to the driver; healthlogic heals after a quiet interval that starts after combat. · log `health`
- **Set wrecked state and release its pilot** (action, `vehiclehits.cpp:482`): Disable hover/autopilot and automatic firing, ask occupants to eject after landing, record crash witness data and preserve native falling momentum.
  - in AddDamage: Add vehicle damage and the driver's share: Damage per hit = native weapon total x player difficulty x [hits] weaponScale (weapondamage.h: a native zero stays zero; [hits] damage=20 only when unreadable). Health 300 by default ([hits] health / limit). DriverShare passes part to the driver; healthlogic heals after a quiet interval that starts after combat. · log `health`
- **Out of combat long enough to repair?** (decision, `vehiclehits.cpp:723`): Combat resets the quiet clock. Wrecks renew the autopilot ban and take longer to begin repairing, then repair more slowly than normal damage.
- **Apply elapsed repair time** (action, `vehiclehits.cpp:726`): Use time since the last update and the quiet gate. Above the repair threshold, clear the wreck, release the autopilot ban and notify the player for their rover.
- **Continue tracking or wait** (state, `vehiclehits.cpp:695`): Concluded projectiles are removed from active tracking. Ineligible damage is ignored; still-busy chassis stay in their current health state.
  - in Tick: Follow shots and native impacts into vehicle hulls: Vehicles take no game damage, so launched projectiles (NoteLaunch from the launch hook) and native impacts (ImpactDetour) are tested against each listed vehicle's oriented hull (interactionlogic::HullHit); the shooter's own vehicle never counts. Also drives the enemy health bar (EnemyBarTick). · log `health`

Arrows: start → source (Process event); source → admit (Native hull attribution); source → track (Tracked launch); track → admit (Hit); track → nohit (No eligible hit); admit → scale (Hostile and new); admit → nohit (Duplicate / friendly / none); scale → accept (Shot damage); other → accept (Ram or other damage); accept → health (Valid); accept → nohit (Refused); health → wreck (New damage total); wreck → break (Yes); wreck → quiet (No); break → quiet (Repair tick); quiet → repair (Yes); quiet → nohit (No); repair → quiet (After next tick)

## Core and Traffic Addon  `#traffic-link`  (Traffic Addon)

Starfield loads each SFSE plugin. The optional addon registers route providers and population callbacks, and the core retains the native boundary.

- **SFSE post-data-load** (state, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:2082`): The addon queries core exports once plugin modules are available. Pre-load invalidates active trip slots.
- **Is the core DLL loaded?** (decision, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:2052`): Attach resolves FlyingVehicleOverhaul.dll and returns when the module is absent.
- **Does the traffic ABI match?** (decision, `api_traffic.cpp:982`): The compiled addon asks for v13 with callbacks. Core rejects other callback versions and preserves v4 as a read-only query without hooks.
  - in ServiceVisibility: Hide or show traffic refs, paced: Hide queue with a token pace; a retirement hide is held while the camera evidence or authority says no. · log `traffic.visibility`
- **Population callbacks unavailable** (state, `api_traffic.cpp:932`): No addon tick or housekeeping registration is accepted. Core ticks its local work independently. Optional provider registration can still proceed if the core module exists.
- **Register addon callbacks** (action, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:2082`): Core copies the hooks and marks the addon attached. Generator receives the adapter and a saved-journey matcher. Everyday scanning is currently a stub.
- **Register route and evidence providers** (action, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:2082`): Exports are negotiated individually. Capability flags feed route admission. Field boxes prefer V2 and fall back to V1. The addon queries core for model, handoff, census and clearance services.
- **Core frame dispatch** (action, `api_traffic.cpp:932`): The core records the player's rover and takeovers, calls registered traffic after the look update, runs Driver Watch, and services visibility.
- **Active save profile available?** (decision, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1962`): SyncProfile returns false when the core supplies no directory. A directory change resets old local state, reloads remembered journeys and binds the working registry.
- **Prepare and tick the pool** (action, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1950`): Refresh route files; rebuild slots on a world, revision, clock or test-position change. Tick the generator, report broken pairs to World and request nearby nav cells.
- **Wait for the next eligible frame** (state, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1981`): During unavailable profile or world preparation, the generator gets no population tick from this entry point.

Arrows: load → core (Attach); core → abi (Yes); core → inactive (No); abi → register (v13); abi → inactive (Incompatible); inactive → providers (Core exports exist); register → providers (Continue); providers → frame (On each core tick); frame → profile (If attached); profile → pool (Yes); profile → wait (No)

## Traffic population and birth  `#traffic-birth`  (Traffic Addon)

The generator builds an authored slot candidate, the population arbiter grants authority, and core listeners validate and execute the asynchronous create request.

- **Read live world and restore census** (action, `traffic/generator.cpp:1368`): Tick requires the core's fleet rail exports and its birth listener and road clearance (a core without them gets no population, logged once), traffic enabled, a presentation, a player with a world, a matching pool and a readable vehicle snapshot. On world changes it reconciles the pool, recovers saved registry identities and cleans up rows of retired systems.
- **Is the birth window open?** (decision, `traffic/spawnflow.h:50`): Normal births open under the loading screen or covered X fill. Normal play and ordinary map views are closed. The diagnostic test-near-player switch opens a play window; unresolved schedule clocks stall filling.
- **Service existing population** (action, `traffic/generator.cpp:1407`): Pair Step and Handoff run before InitialScene. Collection, repair, Health and retirement have their own readiness and authority gates.
- **Eligible slot and route room?** (decision, `traffic/generator.cpp:1264`): InitialScene ranks free authored slots by visibility ring, then distance (rings only order, never refuse). Free eligible slots need fleet room (every slot), a complete occupancy list or the core's road proof, route room and backoff expiry. In play only a real shortfall within the live radius is filled.
- **Eligible model fits?** (decision, `traffic/generator.cpp:1264`): VehicleBase picks from slot model policy with Health and retry state. Measure reads bounds, and the model gate checks lane rule and fit.
- **Can Siting prove this pose?** (decision, `traffic/generator.cpp:1264`): Siting proposes an authored field position; spacing tests known/reserved occupancy. The core's road check is required: unknown or expired proof defers, occupied refuses. Attempts rotate through authored alternatives.
- **Does the population arbiter grant?** (decision, `traffic/spawnflow.h:283`): ArbitratePopulation checks window, profile room, world completeness and siting, then consumes the shared per-tick BirthBudget. Driver repair requests use the same arbiter with their own rule.
- **Core accepts the birth request?** (decision, `api_traffic.cpp:1047`): V1 requires a valid source, base and grant. The road V2 variant binds proof to a chassis request. The listener validates proof, context, concealment and collection vs. start before scheduling work.
- **Queue native work** (action, `lifecycle.cpp:55`): The lifecycle executor requires a compatible service. Work drains via the native service or the reduced covered step. Commands recheck ownership, cover, world, cell and proof; V2 rechecks fresh road occupancy before create.
  - in Submit: Loading screen up and cover thread known?: Needs the native service arena verified (Compatible: 15-byte check at 1422CDF60). Screen up: keep the ticket in the CoverList. Otherwise hand off any cover leftovers, then the native queue.
  - in Submit: Command runs: inside the service (owner thread)?: Wrapped work calls crash::Action, then refuses (result 0) if not InService (threading flag clear or owner == this thread). Request::Run skips canceled or old-epoch work.
- **Did creation return an identity?** (decision, `traffic/generator.cpp:1040`): Pending commands are collected on later calls. Completed chassis identities permit the paired seated-driver request; native refusal is recorded and the slot retries or defers.
- **Reserve and freeze pair for Health** (state, `traffic/generator.cpp:1583`): Health qualifies the current body and seat; Handoff releases the pair to its pilot. The private runtime stock, the factory bay and the below-world staging were removed on 2026-11-21: every birth is a public fleet birth.
- **Leave empty or retry later** (state, `traffic/generator.cpp:1264`): A candidate may rotate to another pose/model, back off, or stay empty until next cover. Ledger records reasons and per-window counts.

Arrows: snapshot → existing (Valid snapshot); existing → window (Start InitialScene); window → room (Open); window → defer (Closed / pending clock); room → model (Eligible); room → defer (No); model → site (Yes); model → defer (No); site → grant (Yes); site → defer (Unknown / refused); grant → listener (Grant); grant → defer (Denied); listener → queue (Start); listener → result (Collect existing); listener → defer (Refused); queue → result (Poll later); result → proving (Accepted or pending); result → defer (Completed refusal)

## Physical fleet, rings and the kept lab  `#traffic-fleet`  (Traffic Addon)

Since 2026-11-21 the Traffic Addon populates with one system: the physical fleet. The core must export the fleet rail functions, its birth listener, road clearance and handoff functions, or the add-on creates no traffic. On world entry the saved fleet is restored. The visibility rings rank which cars get rail paths and which empty slots fill first; they never refuse a car. The lab (factory bay, below-world and runtime stock, seated relocation of stock pairs) is kept in the source but switched off (generator kLabActive=false): no lab slot is made and saved lab rows stay untouched, for later use by the X fill and vehicles leaving a worldspace. Not run in game yet.

- **Bind the core's fleet exports at attach** (action, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:2385`): Attach reads FVO_FleetRailAssignV1 / ReleaseV1 / StateV1 (all three or none), the birth listener and the road clearance exports. Relocation receipts stay optional (diagnostic cores only).
- **Fleet, birth, road and handoff exports present?** (decision, `traffic/generator.cpp:2468` · log `OBS system=traffic.fleet event=refused`): One gate in Tick: without the fleet rail exports, or without the birth listener, road clearance or handoff facts/release, the add-on logs the missing export once and creates no traffic. No second population path exists.
- **No population with this core** (state, `traffic/generator.cpp:2472`): An older core (1.2.4 and before) still loads the add-on (API 13 kept) but gets no traffic from it. The published Traffic Addon 0.3 / 0.3.1 keep working with the new core through the version-13 table.
- **Restore the saved fleet on world entry** (action, `traffic/generator.cpp:1680` · log `OBS system=traffic.census event=fleet_recovered`): Reads the whole world section of the playthrough registry; adopts each valid physical pair once (base check, still a vehicle, not retiring). Unloaded rows wait in the deferred list.
- **Adopt journey-matched linked pairs** (action, `traffic/generator.cpp:1790` · log `OBS system=traffic.census event=fleet_recovery`): A linked driver and vehicle join a free slot only if the same save's journey names the vehicle and world. This is also how an old factory car with a saved journey joins the normal fleet.
- **Viewer reach and visibility rings** (state, `trafficsight.h:48`): T_unseen = create 150 + proving 3600 + margin 100 ms; the viewer's top speed over that time (speed + 6 m/s^2, capped 150 m/s) gives the reach, split into 3 rings. Rings order work; they never refuse a car.
- **Assign rail paths in ring order** (action, `traffic/generator.cpp:2121` · log `OBS system=traffic.fleet event=path_result`): Every 750 ms: driving fleet pairs sorted by ring then distance; FleetPath builds the route points, the population arbiter grants FleetPath, the core's FVO_FleetRailAssignV1 takes the assignment. A car the player took is released.
- **Fill a shortfall slot** (action, `traffic/generator.cpp:2160` · log `OBS system=traffic.live event=grant`): Covered windows (loading screen, X panel) and play: free slots by ring then distance; in play only within the live radius. Arbiter, model fit, Siting, the core's road check and the birth listener; the cap is every slot (the old 32-car live cap is gone).
- **Core rail or native autopilot moves the car** (state, `vehicleflight.cpp:9699`): The core decides per chassis: rail (mode 2) through the shared guarded body writer, awaiting engine evidence (mode 3), or the existing autopilot in native mode. The inspection list shows which.
- **Release assignments on load or world change** (action, `traffic/generator.cpp:2114`): Reset (a save load) and a world change release every rail assignment before restored identities are reused. Reset also clears the model refusal lists and the inspection list (those clears were dead before 2026-11-21).
- **Lab kept, switched off** (state, `traffic/generator.cpp:2511`): kLabActive=false in Reconcile: no factory bay, below-world or runtime stock slot is created, so StepFactory, the stock search, dispatch and relocation of stock pairs never run. The core keeps the lab exports (diagnostic builds) and the lease used by the fast-seat and Numpad 3 tests.

Arrows: bind → guard (Tick); guard → off (missing export); guard → recover (world entry); recover → reach (each tick); journey → reach; reach → paths (driving pairs); reach → fill (empty slots); paths → rail (accepted); fill → rail (proved and handed off); clear → guard (next tick); recover → journey (linked pairs)

## Traffic pair health, handoff and retirement  `#traffic-life`  (Traffic Addon)

Census tracks ownership and lifecycle. Health issues verdicts, Handoff releases staged cars, and the population arbiter authorizes retirement before the native service hides or deletes refs.

- **Recover and update pair census** (action, `traffic/generator.cpp:1264`): Reconcile saved registry and deferred identities. A linked actor alone cannot establish membership; a matching saved journey or persisted identity can restore a pair.
- **Has the player taken the chassis?** (decision, `traffic/generator.cpp:1134`): A player in the driver's seat of a made car can take ownership. Generator marks the pair World and hands a frozen chassis release request to the player path.
- **World / player ownership** (state, `traffic/generator.cpp:1264`): Release may retry until the core accepts it. Retirement checks protected ownership and cancels queued teardown when takeover is observed.
- **What does Health judge?** (decision, `traffic/generator.cpp:665`): Read core driver/body/seat and live-pair facts, apply timers and model/spot evidence, and record a verdict. Recovery can re-enter Driving. Broken pairs reach Retirement, while eligible healthy pairs can proceed to Handoff.
- **Seat, physics and pilot ready?** (decision, `traffic/generator.cpp:1264`): Handoff requires an eligible intact pair, body, loaded/aboard seated driver and compatible core proof. Package, controller binding and recent physics advance the handoff phase; local-road pose may hold release.
- **Did staged release succeed?** (decision, `traffic/generator.cpp:1264`): Core releases the birth hold under recorded authority. Readiness proof and road position may defer it; late failed road pose becomes Health evidence. After release, compatible cores require a fresh physics step before Driving.
- **Driving / journey ownership** (state, `traffic/generator.cpp:1264`): The core driving pipeline controls motion after handoff. Census records the fresh physics or legacy release verdict. An ended trip may request a new one. Vehicle Coordinator handoff admits the command owner before planning. Revocation on takeover or world reset invalidates old grants. The mailbox transports an admitted command and checks freshness without reading Traffic policy.
- **Can the pair retire now?** (decision, `traffic/generator.cpp:608`): Owned references are protected. Other pairs need cover or accepted unseen evidence; the decision accounts for both remaining halves, with special fall/concealment rules.
- **Keep current state and retry** (state, `traffic/generator.cpp:647`): The pair stays staged, recovering, or on a retirement request as appropriate; the next frame supplies new facts.
- **Grant retirement and queue hides** (action, `traffic/generator.cpp:417`): Write the retiring identity to census, tell the core to forget traffic metadata, end any job, hide matching refs and queue the deletion record.
- **Safe to delete the same identity?** (decision, `traffic/generator.cpp:439`): Wait for disable and 3D unload. Revoke on takeover, abandon missing or reused refs as appropriate, and back off explicit engine refusals. Native delete is bounded per update.
- **Close retirement record** (state, `traffic/generator.cpp:1264`): Clear the retiring record when the reference is gone, successfully retired, or the identity cannot safely be acted on. Registry records survive reload for a covered pass.

Arrows: census → taken (Inspect current owner); taken → world (Yes); taken → health (Traffic still owns it); health → mayretire (Broken / removal requested); health → eligible (Eligible pair); health → waiting (Still proving / recovering); eligible → release (Yes); eligible → waiting (No); release → drive (Released and confirmed); release → waiting (Pending / refused); mayretire → hide (Grant); mayretire → waiting (Hold); hide → delete (Await native visibility); delete → waiting (Still loaded / backoff); delete → gone (Completed / close record); delete → world (Takeover)

## Traffic journeys, lane joins and right of way  `#traffic-journey`  (Traffic Addon)

The addon matches routes and tracks trip decisions. Guidance and permissions reach the core as route intent, while occupancy, scheduling and junction rules can hold progression.

- **Receive route request** (state, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1643`): PoolTrip offers a traffic journey and can query demand without reserving. PlanRoute handles a destination marker, PlanJourney validates scripted stops, and PlanCruise is the player's lap entry.
- **Matching remembered vehicle journey?** (decision, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1738`): Begin can restore an identity-matched saved trip after world/base validation rather than pick a new route. It restores route state, updates pose, and returns the step callback.
- **Route and lane fit the request?** (decision, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1694`): Begin checks world, model, initial lane, route room, schedule and live route revision. The destination and script paths plan connected routes; Cruise prefers its prior route within reach and checks hull fit and lane heading.
- **Return no usable claim** (state, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1681`): Reasons include no trip slot, an excluded model, no fitting route, or an unreachable itinerary leg. No controller claim is returned on refusal.
- **Commit trip and route definitions** (action, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1738`): Claim stores lane, leg and route identity; connected routes retain route/link snapshots. Initial staged births can start at their authored station in Follow. The output carries Step and its opaque user slot.
- **Update step and read local rules** (action, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1398`): Step rejects stale claims, updates position and speed, honors explicit turns and pickup wait, then advances the trafficroute leg. Non-hover local travel can move Climb to Join.
- **Is lane entry clear?** (decision, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1445`): A taken join slot prevents Join→Follow or sets a waiting goal at the current XY. A previous join hold clears on leaving Join.
- **Can the route transition proceed?** (decision, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1449`): The next itinerary route is entered through its matching link. An unavailable link at Leave holds. Lane coordination and junction decisions apply holds and speed caps through the provider callbacks.
- **Reached place or script stop?** (decision, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1453`): Parking queues wait overhead for a free space. Script progression waits on arrival/dwell and authored gap, priority or signal conditions; failure to plan the next leg also holds.
- **Publish hold / stop intent** (state, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1453`): The addon returns a hold position or route hold. The core can ask again once the next update supplies changing traffic facts.
- **Publish goal and lane evidence** (action, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1046`): The runtime returns a trip goal and exposes lane windows, travel policy, field geometry and permits. These are inputs to the core planner. Traffic supplies lane geometry, travel permissions, speed, hold reason and stop distance through Route guidance. World supplies obstacles and contacts. Speed and Plan process those inputs through the ordinary driving pipeline.
- **Complete, cycle or request next leg** (state, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:1479`): A completed slot may get a repeated claim via Cycle; scripted work selects the next permitted leg, or ends at the last one.

Arrows: request → restore (Ordinary/pool Begin); request → pick (Destination / script / Cruise); restore → claim (Yes); restore → pick (No); pick → claim (Yes); pick → refuse (No); claim → step (Core calls Step); step → join (Join); step → junction (Follow / Leave); join → hold (Slot occupied); join → guidance (Continue); junction → hold (Link or permit holds); junction → done (Advance); done → hold (Queue / stop not ready); done → guidance (Still following); done → cycle (Completed / ready); guidance → step (Next update); hold → step (Retry)

## Traffic save snapshots and restoration  `#traffic-saves`  (Traffic Addon)

The core owns the game hooks and the save-specific identity. Each load branches into a fresh editable directory, and successful saves publish immutable snapshots.

- **Load or save event** (state, `main_vehicle.cpp:490`): The standalone core installs load and save hooks. The optional shared core API uses its existing before/after-save callbacks.
  - in WithCore: WithCore: run under the core's frame, save and reset callbacks: SetApi, vehicleflight::Install, InstallFeatureSystems, then registers BeforeTrafficSave/AfterTrafficSave, Frame, CorePlayerUpdate (the pulse) and Reset with the core. Legacy path; the core DLL was removed.
- **Clear current profile on load** (action, `trafficprofile.cpp:141`): BeginLoad blocks traffic, clears cached snapshot identity and route path, and allows the same save to branch again after the world is ready.
- **Safe world, core hook and forms ready?** (decision, `main_vehicle.cpp:382`): The update permits access only when the addon is attached and a world/arrival context is safe. Allow additionally needs a working SaveGame hook and warmed forms.
  - in Frame: World safe for the full simulation (!loading && worldReady)?: 2026-11-15 hotfix: no full simulation at the main menu, during a save load or its settle. Door/fast-travel/landing screens without a save load keep the full update. Transitions log and leave a crumb.
  - in Frame: Held: 1 s load trace; re-attach kept vehicles after load: Once a second: loading, world ready, screen, manager state, TES interior cell, hook counts. After the load routine returned and world ready: vehiclediag::AfterLoadTick gives kept vehicles a chassis before the world runs.
  - in Frame: Run Full update, Housekeeping, or nothing?: Full is downgraded to Housekeeping when the world is not safe. Skip runs nothing more except the pacing measurement.
- **Does the save carry a profile ID?** (decision, `trafficprofile.cpp:198`): The core reads the game's saved global. A matching cached identity reuses the current working copy; a save with no ID gets an initial baseline.
- **Create baseline from defaults or prior setup** (action, `trafficprofilestore.h:63`): Create chooses eligible legacy inputs or defaults, then records the initial immutable profile ID in the saved global.
- **Open existing immutable snapshot** (action, `trafficprofilestore.h:23`): Open validates the ID and backing files before cloning it into Working.
- **Activate editable profile and overlay** (action, `trafficprofile.cpp:222`): Clone to Working, synchronize the shared Permanent routes and tell the addon to reset and reload journey/registry state when this directory changes.
- **Traffic paused / report failure** (state, `trafficprofile.cpp:235`): Profile setup and save-hook failures report a reason. An unavailable profile yields an empty directory, so the addon does not start traffic.
- **Can a complete snapshot be saved?** (decision, `trafficprofile.cpp:155`): Core saves its flight state and snapshots traffic only if attached. BeforeSave guards nesting, requires a working directory, flushes external writes and active journeys, and verifies the cached identity.
- **Publish immutable snapshot ID** (action, `trafficprofile.cpp:177`): Clone working routes and sibling registry state into a new immutable snapshot. Set the new ID only after the clone completes and before native serialization; continue editing the same working directory.
- **Call native SaveGame** (action, `main_vehicle.cpp:133`): The game saves the current GLOB. If snapshot preparation failed, logging explains that the previous snapshot ID is serialized while live edits remain active.
  - in SaveGameDetour: SaveGameDetour: record per-save state around the native save: flightsave::OnSave(save name) always; BeforeTrafficSave before the original; AfterTrafficSave from a scope guard after it.

Arrows: enter → reset (Load); reset → ready (Wait for readiness); ready → identity (Yes); ready → paused (No); identity → baseline (No ID); identity → open (Existing ID); identity → working (Cached working copy); baseline → working (Clone); open → working (Clone); open → paused (Missing or invalid snapshot); working → paused (Clone / synchronization fails); enter → save (Save); save → publish (Prepared); save → serialize (Skipped or failed); publish → serialize (New ID)

## Installed content, settings and route data  `#package-data`  (Traffic Addon)

Two enabled ESMs supply game content. Installer route files seed save profiles, and user settings and caches are read from the FVO data folder.

- **Load installed FVO files** (state, `main_vehicle.cpp:511`): The active load order lists FVO_NPCPilots.esm and FVO_CityPlacements.esm. Both match the fvo-hotfix-122/Data copies byte for byte. The current fvo-main CMake builds the DLLs independently.
- **NPC pilot forms resolve?** (decision, `pilotquest.cpp:77`): NPCPilots provides 2 native quests, 512 action packages, and 2 save-persisted globals. Runtime forms resolve plugin name and local ID, which keeps readers independent of the plugin's load-order prefix.
- **Fill pilot and vehicle aliases** (action, `pilotquest.cpp:143`): The core validates the quest and slot before assigning the actor and vehicle. Game quest packages handle boarding and activation. Traffic and ordinary pilot entry points use the relevant assignment paths.
  - in Assign: Send the NPC to the vehicle by quest package: FVO_NPCPilots.esm quest aliases are filled with ForceRefTo so the NPC runs 'activate the vehicle alias' above its schedule. AssignTraffic is the traffic variant; Release frees the alias.
- **Report unavailable form or action** (state, `trafficprofile.cpp:73`): A missing or inactive quest can refuse its assignment. A missing or invalid traffic snapshot global prevents the core from opening the save's route profile.
- **Read per-save globals** (action, `pilotlayout.h:8`): Use 0xD00 for Traffic Manager first-time state and 0xD01 for the saved traffic profile identity. The game persists both. The core resolves them before taking the file lock.
- **Register loaded native placements** (action, `realvehicles.cpp:639`): CityPlacements supplies 90 native references in 27 cell records and 9 worldspace records. Starfield loads the placements from the ESM. The core registers recognized vehicles as their cells appear; traffic fleet creation has a separate population path.
- **Resolve FVO user data folder** (action, `fvopaths.h:12`): The core locates Documents/My Games/Starfield/SFSE/Logs/FlyingVehicleOverhaul. When needed, it migrates the old SeamlessVehicle folder and seamless.ini or uses the old folder if the move fails.
- **Load options and UI settings** (action, `features.cpp:68`): The feature registry applies available installer choice changes at startup, then reads settings.ini. Separate readers handle bindings.ini, hoverui.ini and routeprobes.ini. The installed .features directory currently contains only the Vortex marker.
- **Does this save already have a route profile?** (decision, `trafficprofile.cpp:198`): Once a save can use traffic, a valid stored identity opens that immutable profile. A new identity comes from installer defaults beside the core DLL. Existing traffic snapshots are covered in the Traffic save snapshots view.
- **Seed the first immutable profile** (action, `trafficprofilestore.h:63`): Copy eligible .ini and .txt files from SFSE/Plugins/FVO_Traffic. A one-time established-save import can include editor and vehicle-group routes and carry the old fleet and ring-road files. Ready is written after the copy completes.
- **Read working route files** (action, `traffic/traffic_main.cpp:230`): The addon watches the editable route directory, parses sorted INIs, adds ring-road routes, gives editor versions precedence and applies lane defaults. The imported directories also hold saved journey and pair state.
- **Write save sidecars and diagnostics** (state, `flightsave.cpp:33`): Runtime data includes FlightStates, TrafficProfiles/Working and Permanent, world navcache, vehicle and placement registries and diagnostic files. These are generated data. The 79-byte installed FVO_UITest.swf is a retired diagnostic asset.

Arrows: installed → pilots (NPCPilots ESM); installed → placed (CityPlacements ESM); installed → folder (Core DLL); pilots → assign (Quest ready); pilots → globals (Read profile / introduction globals); pilots → formhold (Required forms unavailable); folder → settings (Gameplay configuration); folder → saved (TrafficProfile storage); globals → saved (Snapshot identity); saved → seed (No saved profile yet); saved → routefiles (Restore working copy); saved → formhold (Invalid or missing profile data); seed → routefiles (Clone working copy); routefiles → outputs (Persistence and diagnostics)

## Native movement controller and probe  `#native-research`  (Native services and streaming)

The native planner adapter and the read-only probe are present in the build source list. The current runtime update calls neither. The graph records their retained behavior for source review.

- **FVO runtime update** (state, `main_vehicle.cpp:333`): This build ticks the active FVO flight and loaded-area systems. The historical controller lookup was removed from this loop after the test showed an engine controller for each vehicle and incurred lock cost.
  - in Update: 6 flight: vehicleflight::Tick, vehiclediag::Tick, loadedarea::Tick: Cost bucket 5. The physics itself runs later in the vehicle-step hook on the physics thread; this is the main-thread planning side.
  - in Update: Vehicle update enters area D: Order in the full update: npcvehicles::BeginFrame, vehiclehits::Tick, (map/nav), npcvehicles::Tick + passenger::Tick, vehicleflight::Tick, then realvehicles, ringroad, trafficpool::Tick, scantarget, traffic::Tick, vehiclekeep::Tick. On loading-screen arrival frames only world::Publish, navseed and trafficpool::Tick run.
- **Read-only probe tick** (action, `native_movement_probe.cpp:118`): Historical diagnostic path, with no runtime call in this source. It checks the expected engine functions, samples the player and a bounded near-vehicle list on a five-second interval, and never submits goals.
- **Read native component and release handle** (action, `native_movement_probe.cpp:97`): Uses a counted native lookup, reports component and owner identity, goal state and stored output, then releases the handle. Missing or faulted results are reported.
- **Exact native ABI fingerprints match?** (decision, `native_movement_engine.cpp:46`): The unused adapter checks function bytes and table entries and binds a stable image base. A mismatch returns an empty API.
- **Can open a private controller?** (decision, `native_movement_controller.cpp:62`): Requires a complete API, measured hull bounds, an eligible non-actor reference and pathing object. Builds a private controller, supplies hull dimensions, activates its normal configuration and verifies the required interfaces.
- **Goal request accepted?** (decision, `native_movement_controller.cpp:104`): Cancels the previous request before trying a new one. Invalid destination/missing construction is unavailable; rejected enqueue clears the setter and becomes blocked; accepted work becomes waiting.
- **Read native arbiter state** (decision, `native_movement_controller.cpp:138`): Only waiting/following runs a finite positive bounded update. The arbiter reports waiting, following, arrived, blocked or unavailable. A stale/replaced output interface publishes no motion.
- **Decode fresh planner output** (action, `native_movement_output.h:63`): Requires valid finite output and nonnegative speed. Combines current rotation and planner angle delta, with pitch-derived lift only for flight, and returns motion to the hypothetical caller.
- **Clear goal and close private controller** (state, `native_movement_controller.cpp:175`): Cancellation advances generation. Close restores the original data table after cancellation, then destroys and clears the owned controller.
- **Unavailable or inactive** (state, `native_movement_engine.cpp:60`): The runtime has no caller for the adapter and the resolver does not supply the optional destination request builder. These are source paths for review and test, with no live FVO movement authority.

Arrows: runtime → off (Current runtime excludes both); history → handle (Probe when compatible); resolve → open (Compatible); resolve → off (Mismatch); open → request (Ready); open → off (Unavailable); request → update (Accepted); request → stop (Rejected / cancel); update → update (Waiting); update → motion (Following with fresh output); update → stop (Arrived / blocked / unavailable); motion → update (Next eligible update); motion → stop (Close)

## Native placements, registry and game guards  `#native-placements`  (Native services and streaming)

The core scans cells incrementally, registers recognizable references, and only pursues legacy cleanup through ownership checks. Separately, guarded game hooks handle local-map limits, actor disable readiness and dust creation.

- **Refresh available vehicle and placement data** (action, `realvehicles.cpp:688`): Every new registry scan discovers real vehicle bases and checks the optional Parked Vehicles forms, loads legacy placement IDs and begins a bounded scan of loaded cells.
  - in Tick: Vehicle registry and road inventory: Records every real vehicle in loaded cells with place, owner and user (vehicles.tsv), clears stacked duplicates; RefreshRoadInventory/AppendRoadVehicles give the addon the road's existing vehicles; placements and summon points. · log `traffic.road_inventory`
- **Recognized enabled vehicle?** (decision, `realvehicles.cpp:639`): ScanCell requires valid reference and base. A real vehicle with enabled state can populate the world registry. With a resolved Parked Vehicles source, eligible parked duplicates get checked separately.
- **Register pose, model and identity** (action, `realvehicles.cpp:455`): Read and record recognized vehicles as loaded. The registry can feed World and road inventory readers. A dirtied registry is saved periodically.
- **An eligible duplicate or legacy runtime ref?** (decision, `realvehicles.cpp:612`): Optional Parked Vehicles cleanup checks matching props and stacked parked vehicles. Old runtime-spawned IDs are also checked when loaded; reused IDs, plugin-native placements and player/NPC-owned candidates follow their respective guards.
- **Does parked retirement permit hiding?** (decision, `api_traffic.cpp:940`): Core checks protected identities and uncollected chassis at MayHideParked. The attached Traffic Addon can arbitrate; core-only stays with its identity check.
- **Call native disable for allowed ref** (action, `realvehicles.cpp:426`): A granted unprotected duplicate or legacy reference is disabled if the native call succeeds. Legacy placement records are updated only when that work is resolved.
- **Keep or defer existing identity** (state, `realvehicles.cpp:612`): Continue the bounded scan. Unloaded, active or refused legacy references may be reconsidered on a later scan; stale wrong-base entries are dropped from the old tracking list.
- **Placement authoring routes dormant** (state, `realvehicles.cpp:719`): RecordingPlacements returns false and feature availability excludes Placement and PlacementMode. The compiled scanner edit functions are gated by that false value. Game-authored/ESM references are still registered.
- **Install compatible map and disable guards** (action, `main_vehicle.cpp:477`): The core installs these separately. The cap requires the expected map-task entry, the dust hook requires its create-reference signature, and the actor-disable hook requires its own matching prologue.
  - in InstallFeatureSystems: InstallFeatureSystems: every feature hook, once: world::SetServices (vehicle list callbacks), then hoverui, routeedit, mapguard, disableguard, inputgate, mappoints, loadedarea, npcvehicles, profiles, vehicleaim, vehiclediag, vehiclehits enemy bar, flighttune::Load. Each Install checks its own bytes and logs on mismatch; none can stop the service.
- **Actor ready for native disable?** (decision, `disableguard.cpp:35`): The guard forwards normal calls. It skips a non-null actor whose setup is missing or faults when inspected, recording a bounded diagnostic.
- **Local map build exceeds 60,000 refs?** (decision, `mapguard.cpp:102`): Large task vectors are temporarily capped before calling the original map build. The original end is restored for ownership and cleanup. Smaller tasks call through with their entire vector.
  - in RunDetour: Local map model build over 60000 references?: Vtable slot patch on the build task (0x144C54E38 slot 2, FUN_14142FAE0). Over kMaxModels 60000 the list is shortened for the build and restored after (the node's child count is 16-bit; 65535 wrapped and crashed). · log `OBS system=map.trace`
- **Wheel dust is near and eligible?** (decision, `mapguard.cpp:312`): Create-reference filters recognized dust outside the configured radius and suppresses persistent-cell dust even nearby. Valid non-persistent dust and unrelated creates call through.
- **Forward eligible native work** (action, `mapguard.cpp:443`): Actor disable, map construction and reference creation retain their original native paths after their separate gates. The map tick avoids reporting during load.
  - in Tick: mapguard::Tick: persistent-cell report, dust settings: Any non-Skip work. Skipped while loading. Resolves dust bases, reads [world] dustNear (120 m), reports the persistent cell every 60 s or after a build over 20000.
- **Manual saved-dust sweep dormant** (state, `mapguard.cpp:377`): The compiled helper has no active tick caller. Native creation filtering and periodic persistent-cell diagnostics remain live. The persistent-cell rewrite option is false.

Arrows: scan → lookup (Each reference); lookup → register (Enabled vehicle); lookup → legacy (Optional known prop or legacy scan); lookup → keep (Unrelated / invalid); register → legacy (Check duplicate and legacy cases); legacy → authority (Candidate); legacy → keep (No candidate); authority → hide (Granted); authority → keep (Rejected / defer); scan → parkedoff (Authoring gate always false); checks → ready (Actor disable hook); checks → cap (Local map hook); checks → dust (Reference-create hook); checks → dustoff (Existing dust cleanup off); ready → native (Ready); ready → keep (Unready); cap → native (Call original with applicable cap); dust → native (Eligible or unrelated); dust → keep (Suppress dust create)

## Streaming, visibility and reference position  `#native-streaming`  (Native services and streaming)

The core handles separate mechanisms for movement beyond the loaded grid, distance fade and visibility retention. Traffic reads visibility evidence before birth and retirement.

- **Publish flown references and body IDs** (action, `loadedarea.cpp:859`): The loaded-area tick lists FVO-flown NPC chassis and drivers and the player's own vehicle during FVO autopilot. It refreshes body mappings on a timer and watches driver identity.
- **Eligible loaded-area boundary interaction?** (decision, `loadedarea.cpp:202`): With WallPass enabled, the hooks filter matching side touches and collision pairs and suppress matching move-back. The floor and unrelated bodies retain normal handling. Invalid or detached body owners are rejected separately.
- **Can reconcile body to reference?** (decision, `loadedarea.cpp:572`): With WallPass active, ensure persistence and check component, live body owner and current scene. Ignore sub-metre gaps and refuse a jump over 500 m. The measured body position supplies the target.
- **Target crosses the current grid cell?** (decision, `loadedarea.cpp:601`): A valid move within the cell uses the game's position setter and shifts the seated driver and model when applicable. A crossing records the target for a queued cell move.
- **Refiling through lifecycle service** (action, `loadedarea.cpp:666`): Queue one keyed move per identity. Acquire the live reference and run the cell refiling in the allowed service, with native cell/readiness checks.
- **Leave reference or native behavior alone** (state, `loadedarea.cpp:880`): Unlisted bodies, disabled WallPass, invalid scenes and refused/unchanged body positions do not produce a streaming position correction.
- **Synchronize valid position** (action, `loadedarea.cpp:612`): The native position setter updates the chassis reference; movement carries a seated driver and updates the model root when available. A planner still owns the desired travel.
- **Evaluate visibility retention** (decision, `vehiclekeep.cpp:396`): Once the world settles, clean prior journal flags and scan loaded candidates. Every 250 ms consider same-world, view cone, edge distance, active use, traffic birth reservations, Health, and native Full LOD state.
- **Grant or retain Full LOD** (action, `vehiclekeep.cpp:348`): Prioritize busy vehicles and pace hold transitions. Born traffic has its own reservation budget; native Full LOD is excluded. Record flags the mod set for later cleanup.
- **Release an owned hold** (action, `vehiclekeep.cpp:323`): Release invalid/disabled/other-world refs. Short no-cell gaps can retain a hold; prolonged lack of a cell releases it. Obscured broken pairs may release past the edge, and decorative holds can expire out of sight.
- **Return camera evidence to Traffic** (action, `vehiclekeep.cpp:538`): Sample fresh gameplay camera and mouse turn. A likely imminent view can stall births. The retirement reader uses model size and camera cone/forecast before requesting bounded visibility evidence.
- **Apply configured object/actor distance fade** (action, `viewdist.cpp:62`): Watch [view] settings and refresh the game's table when values change. Default object fade zero uses the existing no-distance-fade path for loaded objects; Full LOD retention is handled separately.
  - in Tick: View distance: apply [view] objectFade / actorFade: Writes fFadeOutMultObjects/Actors and calls the game's table rebuild FUN_140573d40, checked at most once a second; defaults objects 0 (fade off), actors 1. A failed write disables it.

Arrows: tick → walls (Current body list); tick → sync (WallPass active); walls → unchanged (Unlisted / off); walls → sync (Eligible movement continues); sync → unchanged (Unavailable / tiny or invalid step); sync → cell (Matching live body and target); cell → queue (Crossing); cell → follow (Same cell); tick → vis (Parallel core visibility tick); vis → held (Qualified / keep); vis → drop (Existing hold no longer eligible); vis → unchanged (Candidate excluded); tick → forecast (Parallel traffic look input); tick → fade (Parallel core distance tick)

## Traffic Manager and photo mode  `#photo-editor`  (Interface and authoring)

F8 uses the native photo-mode transition to host the Traffic Manager. F7 is the default HUD editor binding.

- **World-safe frame** (state, `main_vehicle.cpp:426`): The main task services photo entry and exit, ground probes and the route-item scanner during admitted work with a safe world.
  - in Frame: Frame(): main-thread task entry each SFSE task pass: trafficprofile::Report first (outside the re-entry guard), then the guard, heartbeat, load drop, world state, scheduler.
  - in Frame: World safe for the full simulation (!loading && worldReady)?: 2026-11-15 hotfix: no full simulation at the main menu, during a save load or its settle. Door/fast-travel/landing screens without a save load keep the full update. Transitions log and leave a crumb.
  - in Frame: Behind the screen during a save settle: vehicle list + traffic only: When arrival && loading && work != Skip: world::Publish, navseed::Tick (nav map seeded first), trafficpool::Tick. Flight, AI and everything else wait.
- **Can F8 request photo mode?** (decision, `routeedit.cpp:6865`): Require a focused F8 edge, working photo hook, attached addon, no recent map overlay, allowed gameplay context and a UI pointer.
- **Wait for another frame** (state, `routeedit.cpp:6881`): A missing edge or denied gate leaves the editor unopened. Clear the pending request after three seconds if the native menu did not open.
- **Request native photo mode** (action, `routeedit.cpp:6894`): Queue an editor-open intent and call the native photo-mode entry function.
- **Native photo menu opens** (state, `routeedit.cpp:217`): The detour records the host movie and increments the session ID after native menu handling.
- **Can the overlay render?** (decision, `routeedit.cpp:5714`): Read the camera, reuse the last good photo snapshot for up to 300 ms, and require attached addon, working profile and matching movie.
- **Hide or skip overlay** (state, `routeedit.cpp:5775`): Invalid camera or unavailable context hides the overlay. An overlay build failure disables that movie's overlay until reload.
- **Read-only map overview** (state, `routeedit.cpp:5802`): The surface map draws MapOverview and returns. Photo mode hosts editor input.
  - in Hide: Hide overlay, release photo editor input: The common exit when nothing may be shown.
- **Open or close the editor?** (decision, `routeedit.cpp:5822`): A new photo visit consumes the entry intent. Ordinary photo visits start closed. Later F8 or right-stick click toggles while loading is inactive.
- **Load world and profile** (action, `routeedit.cpp:827`): Require world name and working traffic directory. Reload saved routes and groups when changing world or profile.
- **Camera hold active?** (decision, `routeedit.cpp:5749`): RMB or L3 enables movement while focused with no text entry, load or active route drag. Wait for held inputs to clear before editing resumes.
- **Pass selected camera controls** (action, `photoeditorinput.h:8`): Pass movement, mouse look, wheel, Ctrl+RMB lift and pad sticks/triggers as applicable. Other native photo actions stay blocked.
- **Edit with the overlay pointer** (action, `routeedit.cpp:5803`): Read raw mouse deltas for the overlay cursor. While editing, suppress the native photo controls and route editor clicks to the active draft.
  - in Hide: Hide overlay, release photo editor input: The common exit when nothing may be shown.
- **Recover overlay fault** (action, `routeedit.cpp:6986`): Restore UI and input, clear gestures and queue native photo exit. Keep the draft, including on recoverable gesture faults.
- **Close editor or photo mode** (action, `routeedit.cpp:5830`): Right-stick click closes the editor and keeps photo mode open. F8 closes the editor and queues native photo exit on the main task.

Arrows: frame → gate (Check key); gate → wait (No edge or gate refused); gate → open (Allowed); open → menu (Native visit opened); open → wait (Opening timed out); menu → host (Movie advances); host → hide (Unavailable); host → map (Surface map); host → toggle (Photo host); toggle → draft (Open or stay open); toggle → hide (Closed); toggle → exit (Toggle to closed); draft → gesture (Editor available); gesture → camera (RMB or L3 held); gesture → edit (Editing resumes); camera → toggle (Next movie advance); edit → toggle (Next movie advance); edit → fault (Overlay exception); fault → exit (Native exit requested)

## Route placement, testing and saving  `#route-authoring`  (Interface and authoring)

The editor stages route, lane, place and group changes. Save validates and updates the active working profile. Permanent Save and Permanent Delete operate on shared routes.

- **Route editor action** (state, `routeedit.cpp:4161`): Select an editor command for routes, links, lanes, groups or places.
- **What kind of placement?** (decision, `routeedit.cpp:1294`): A ring moves its centre. A click within 12 pixels of an existing projected line inserts a point at interpolated height. Other clicks place against the cursor's surface or a height plane.
- **Raise or await ground** (action, `routeedit.cpp:1330`): Place 2 m over the first surface hit when available. Otherwise use the height plane with terrain or down-ray checks. A later answer can still raise a matching point.
  - in DropPoint: Drop a point (after selected / into a stretch / at end): Placed at once at the baseline height, then moved to 2 m above the ray answer when GroundTick returns. PlacedHeight never lowers a point. The tile's primitive is carried to the new point. · log `OBS routeedit.place`
- **Are coordinates and terrain valid?** (decision, `routeclearance.h:9`): Bad coordinates and proved terrain violations can block placement. Intentional structure intersections can produce a red warning and remain editable.
  - in Judge: Point under known terrain or invalid?: Only a proven terrain violation or invalid coordinate blocks; mapped structures (hangar, tunnel) only warn. Context edits check only points they add or move (2026-11-11).
- **Report refusal** (state, `routeedit.cpp:1346`): Reject the invalid point or a failed pre-write validation and display the relevant status.
- **Update draft and related data** (action, `routeedit.cpp:1347`): When inserting a point, update section, road, spline and lane metadata and the route links. A late ground answer only adjusts a matching point.
- **Restore last saved editor state** (action, `routeedit.cpp:1005`): Cancel clears draft and staging selections, restores saved route file and groups, and refreshes surrounding routes.
  - in Save: Route groups changed? Write VehicleGroups.ini: Same atomic write; failure reports 'Routes saved, but not the route groups'.
- **Mark the route for a test run** (action, `routeedit.cpp:4684`): Set the test flag and call normal Save. On success, the editor reports a one-run request for the next eligible nearby vehicle with a driver.
  - in EditDay: Time rules: busy words, windows, traffic level bands: Planet local time from planet+0xEF8 (preview hour does not change the clock). Level bands are vehicles per km, clamped to fvolimits::kVehicles. · log `OBS system=routeedit.traffic_level`
- **Does Save pass validation?** (decision, `routeedit.cpp:940`): Lock profile files and check active playthrough, shape, schedule and enabled geometry. Protect editor-external files. Convert anchored coordinates for disk.
  - in LoadGroups: Load vehicle groups (VehicleGroups.ini + owned permanent files): routeGroups and routeGroupsSaved; groups are written only when they changed.
- **Local or permanent destination?** (decision, `routeedit.cpp:978`): Normal Save writes the working file. Permanent Save requires a selected route and access to shared storage.
  - in Save: Permanent Save of the selected route?: Needs a draft of a route at a valid index and shared storage (trafficprofile::PermanentDir).
- **Publish selected route overlay** (action, `routeedit.cpp:980`): Write the selected shared entry and context. If a new permanent file would replace a disabled shared file, write beside it. Refresh overlays and remove the staged local duplicate.
  - in Save: Permanent Save of the selected route?: Needs a draft of a route at a valid index and shared storage (trafficprofile::PermanentDir).
- **Write working profile** (action, `routeedit.cpp:991`): Atomically replace the route file and write modified groups separately. Success clears the draft and undo history and refreshes other routes.
  - in Save: Route groups changed? Write VehicleGroups.ini: Same atomic write; failure reports 'Routes saved, but not the route groups'.
- **Confirm Permanent Delete?** (decision, `routeedit.cpp:1004`): Require an unchanged working profile, a known shared route and a second matching click within three seconds.
  - in Save: Permanent Save of the selected route?: Needs a draft of a route at a valid index and shared storage (trafficprofile::PermanentDir).
- **Remove shared route entry** (action, `routeedit.cpp:1004`): Rewrite the shared overlay and refresh the working copy. Existing local overrides and prior snapshots retain their own records.
- **Report the write result** (state, `routeedit.cpp:991`): Display the saved or failed status. A late failure after a shared overlay or local route write reports partial completion.
  - in Save: Route groups changed? Write VehicleGroups.ini: Same atomic write; failure reports 'Routes saved, but not the route groups'.

Arrows: tool → target (Point placement); target → surface (Free point); target → check (Split existing line); target → draft (Move ring centre); surface → check (Candidate position); check → refuse (Invalid); check → draft (Allowed, including advisory red); tool → draft (Other edits); tool → cancel (Cancel); tool → test (Test); test → validate (Save test flag); tool → validate (Save or Permanent Save); validate → refuse (Failed checks); validate → targetfile (Valid); targetfile → local (Local save); targetfile → shared (Permanent save); shared → local (Shared write and refresh succeed); tool → confirm (Permanent Delete); confirm → remove (Confirmed); confirm → refuse (Unavailable or awaiting second click); shared → status (Shared write or refresh fails); local → status (Local write result); remove → status (Shared deletion result)

## Input ownership, gestures and HUD editing  `#input-routing`  (Interface and authoring)

The binding sampler publishes context-scoped commands. The game's input resolver runs first and then applies the mod's block request when ownership applies.

- **Poll inputs and settings** (state, `bindings.cpp:387`): Poll the pad, load bindings.ini, and track fresh device input to select displayed labels.
- **Is mod input eligible?** (decision, `bindings.cpp:405`): Require a usable world, allowed focused control context, suppression released and no active remap capture. Scope actions to the current vehicle, passenger and scanner layer.
  - in Tick: World present (not loading, not main menu)?: 2026-11-15 hotfix: with world=false the player and vehicle are not read, FVO input is off and no key is taken from the game.
- **Wait for eligible input** (state, `bindings.cpp:451`): Suppress gestures across changes in context or eligibility. Release bound keys and controller buttons after load before input resumes.
- **Resolve scoped gestures** (action, `commandinput.h:38`): Filter raw, tap, hold and double-tap candidates. A compatible double binding delays its competing tap.
  - in Update: Resolve raw / tap / hold 0.5 s / double 0.3 s per alternative: Per alternative a GestureState; chord modifiers (Pad:LB+X) keep plain bindings of other buttons quiet; a double-bound input delays its tap to tell a double. A context or allowed change cancels gestures and requires a fresh press.
- **Publish command edges** (action, `bindings.cpp:421`): Buffer starts and triggers, publish them during the gameplay update and clear delivered edges after readers run.
- **Publish native block requests** (action, `bindings.cpp:426`): Claim mod alternatives that are outside the native default and the passenger's movement, boost and jump controls.
  - in Tick: Mark keys / pad bits the mod owns now: For every live action alternative in context: its VK and pad bit are blocked from the game unless they follow the game's own binding for that action (Boost on RB counts as native; DriverExit is not native while hovering). Riding as passenger also blocks W A S D, Space and Shift.
- **Resolve a native input event** (state, `inputgate.cpp:157`): Run the game's native resolver and then inspect the resulting event.
- **Is editor policy active?** (decision, `inputgate.cpp:79`): The photo editor captures mouse motion and gates its own controls. The map editor and wheel capture each have their own guard paths.
  - in Gate: Game input queue: owned press gets block reason 4: E8 redirect of the resolver call at 0x022DAC60 (-> FUN_1422F1840). After resolving, a press the mod owns gets +0x34 = 4 so no game handler matches. inputownership::Own keeps ownership through the release even if menus or modes change; a native block reason is never replaced. Also stamps fresh device presses and accumulates look-mouse deltas.
- **Does FVO own this button?** (decision, `inputgate.cpp:132`): Check the requested mask and current mod key eligibility. Track ownership across repeats and release, including across a context change.
  - in Gate: Game input queue: owned press gets block reason 4: E8 redirect of the resolver call at 0x022DAC60 (-> FUN_1422F1840). After resolving, a press the mod owns gets +0x34 = 4 so no game handler matches. inputownership::Own keeps ownership through the release even if menus or modes change; a native block reason is never replaced. Also stamps fresh device presses and accumulates look-mouse deltas.
- **Suppress the owned action** (action, `inputgate.cpp:149`): Set FVO's block reason only if the editor policy or binding ownership applies and the engine supplied no reason.
  - in Gate: Game input queue: owned press gets block reason 4: E8 redirect of the resolver call at 0x022DAC60 (-> FUN_1422F1840). After resolving, a press the mod owns gets +0x34 = 4 so no game handler matches. inputownership::Own keeps ownership through the release even if menus or modes change; a native block reason is never replaced. Also stamps fresh device presses and accumulates look-mouse deltas.
- **Preserve native action or reason** (state, `inputgate.cpp:151`): Unclaimed events follow native handling. An existing native block reason takes precedence.
  - in Gate: Game input queue: owned press gets block reason 4: E8 redirect of the resolver call at 0x022DAC60 (-> FUN_1422F1840). After resolving, a press the mod owns gets +0x34 = 4 so no game handler matches. inputownership::Own keeps ownership through the release even if menus or modes change; a native block reason is never replaced.
